# Comsky Books MCP server: build specification

> **Status:** design, decision-complete, **nothing built**. Written 2026-10-22 by the architect pass against `HEAD` = `83d9876` plus the uncommitted parity-wave-3 tree.
> **Two passes:** the first pass wrote sections 0 to 4 and stopped (a usage limit); the second wrote sections 5 to 8 and Appendices A to E, re-verified the claims of the first against the repo, the npm registry and the SDK's own documentation (Appendix B lists what was re-read and what could not be), and corrected what it found: a wrong line citation for the device-id pattern, an over-stated `fromJsonSchema` tag, a gap in period presets, an ambiguous reference, a body-limit claim the SDK docs do not support, and a principal-carrier note that now cites the factory's `authInfo`.
> **Third pass (review round, 2026-10-22):** two adversarial lenses (security; correctness and protocol) returned 44 findings (3 blockers, 23 majors, 18 minors). Each was re-checked against the repo, the MCP 2026-07-28 pages, the SDK docs and the installed `pg` driver source before it was applied; Appendix F is the **Review log** (what was applied, what was applied with a correction, what was only partly adopted and why). The changes that matter most: the key-and-policy lookup no longer joins with `p.*` (a column collision could resolve a key to the wrong tenant); MCP keys are read-scope keys approved by an owner or admin, and MCP writes no longer ride the REST `write` scope (D22); the invoice builder now snapshots tax with the app's own `resolveItemGst` and `snapshotOf` instead of the item's own record (2.4.3); the preview token is described as an integrity check and the draft plus ceilings are the control (D5, D21); ledger creation left v1, so **v1 has 16 tools** (D13); the loader projects an allowlist instead of deleting a blocklist (D11); compute leaves the sync server's event loop (D20); a tenant is disabled unless Comhard allows it (D14); the server itself refuses plain HTTP (D15); a replayed confirm is answered before validation and survives a promotion (D6).
> **Reading order.** Everyone: section 0. **Core lane:** 1.4, 1.5, 2, 4, 5, 6, 7.3 and 7.4, Appendices A and D. **Remote lane:** 1.1, 1.6, 2.4.5, 3, 4, 6.2, 6.9, 7.3 to 7.5, Appendices C and E. **Owner:** 0.1, 0.2, 7.1, 7.6 and section 8.
> **Question it answers:** how do customers connect Claude Desktop, Claude Code, Cursor, claude.ai, ChatGPT and other MCP clients to *their own* Comsky Books data, ask questions, get real reports and (carefully) create entries?
> **Inputs read in full:** `docs/research/mcp-landscape.md`, `mcp-data-audit.md`, `mcp-accounting-use-cases.md`, and the code they cite (`backend/src/{rest,platform,restPolicy,server,db,numbering,sync,conflict,migrate,config,logging,tenancy,subscription,ratelimit,realtime}.js`, `backend/sql/001,002,006,008`, the `src/core` engines named in section 1.4, `src/ui/VoucherScreen.tsx` save path, `src/state/store.ts` numbering, `scripts/perf/run.mjs`, `scripts/seed/*`, `scripts/pg/*`).
> **Who builds it:** two lanes (core, remote) on disjoint files, section 7. **Where it lives:** a new top-level folder `mcp/`, one new backend file `backend/src/mcpRoute.js`, one registration line in `backend/src/server.js` that the orchestrator applies later. No existing source file is edited by this design.

**Evidence tags.** `[C file:line]` read in this repo. `[W]` an official page I fetched this session (URL in Appendix B; paraphrased, never copied). `[R]` carried from the three research docs, whose authors fetched the page (I did not re-fetch it). `[Ws]` a search summary only. `[J]` my judgement. `[unverified]` could not be checked, **including every statement about how a live client behaves: no MCP client, no Postgres on the VM and no customer data was touched for this document.** Line numbers are working-tree numbers of 2026-10-22; grep the identifier before relying on one.

---

## 0. Read this first

### 0.1 The decisions, in one table

Each row is binding on the builders. "Veto" means the owner may overrule it (section 8 has the question and the default the build proceeds on).

| # | Decision | Why (short) | Veto |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | **One tool library, three data sources.** `mcp/` holds the tools and a `DataSource` interface; implementations are `PgDataSource` (inside the backend, reads Postgres directly), `RestDataSource` (stdio and standalone HTTP, talks to the backend) and `FileDataSource` (a company backup file, read-only). | The research proposed local compute (landscape), server compute (use-cases) and backup mode (audit). One interface serves all three and the tools never know which. | no |
| D2 | **Tools compute on an in-memory snapshot of one company** (the engines' own input arrays), cached per tenant and company with a TTL and an incremental refresh by change-log `seq`. No tool runs SQL per question. | The numbers must equal the app's, and the app's numbers come from the pure engines over the voucher log. | no |
| D3 | **The write path is server-authoritative.** Planner, validator, token and commit logic live in `mcp/src/write/` but are only ever *executed* inside the backend (`InProcessWriteGateway`). The stdio entry reaches it through two gateway routes, `write/preview` and `write/commit` (Appendix E). A client never sends a document, only an intent. | Only the backend can reserve a number, hold the tenant lock, stamp the key and re-validate in one transaction. `POST /v1` validates nothing [C rest.js:58-76]. | no |
| D4 | **AI-created vouchers land as Optional (draft), always.** v1 has no way to post a Regular voucher. A person promotes a draft in the app (Alt+L on the voucher in a browser tab, Ctrl+L in the desktop app, or Mark Regular over a selection). | An Optional voucher is recorded and excluded from every total, return and balance [C accounting.ts:9]. A wrong or injected entry cannot move a figure. **This, not the token of D5, is the control that bounds a wrong or injected write**, and D21 bounds how many drafts there can be. | yes |
| D5 | **Preview, then confirm: an integrity check, not a human approval.** `books_preview_voucher` is read-only and returns the exact balanced document, the GST split and a signed, expiring token bound to a hash of that document, the key and the idempotency key. `books_confirm_voucher` takes the *same intent again plus the token*. No token, no write. | A stateless token (no new table) that makes "different from what was previewed" and "another key's token" detectable, and a confirm call whose arguments are the intent. **It does not prove that a person looked:** the same model calls both tools, and a client that auto-approves read-only tools shows the person nothing. What bounds a wrong or injected write is D4 (a draft moves no figure) and D21 (ceilings); a real approval step (MCP elicitation, 4.1 item 7) is specified for v1.1. | no |
| D6 | **Idempotency by key id plus caller key, recorded in a side table.** Every preview carries an `idempotency_key`; the stored document id is derived from (tenant, company, **key id**, caller key), and every commit is recorded in `mcp_commit` (document id, payload hash, number, seq), which survives a person editing or promoting the draft. A retry (even after the token expired, even after the draft was promoted) is answered from `mcp_commit` **before any validation** and returns the same voucher; the same key with a different payload is refused. | `POST /v1` without an id creates a second voucher on retry [C rest.js:64]; the document's own `origin` is dropped by the first edit in the app (2.4.5), so it cannot be the replay record; and validation runs duplicate checks that the draft itself would trip on a retry. | no |
| D7 | **Numbers are reserved at confirm, in the same transaction as the insert**, through the wave-3 `reserveNumber` called in-process. A draft takes a real number from the series (the app does the same for Optional vouchers [C store.ts:719-735]); a draft that is deleted leaves a gap. Hand-numbered voucher types are refused. | An API key cannot call `/sync/next-number` [C sync.js:319-325]; in-process we can, atomically. | no |
| D8 | **Attribution:** `change_log.device_id = 'mcp:<key id, first 8>'`, `user_id` = the person who created the key, the document carries an `origin` object and a short `[AI draft]` narration tag. **These stamps are hints, not evidence:** any device can push any device id that matches the pattern [C conflict.js:94, sync.js:182] and any REST write can set `origin`; the authoritative record of what the MCP wrote is `mcp_commit` (2.4.5) and the audit rows, and outputs label `origin` as *claimed*. The app's Edit Log cannot show it today (it reads the local outbox only [C AuditLog.tsx:101]); the app change that would is named, not made (section 7.5, seam A1). | The stated aim "the audit log shows `MCP: <key name>`" needs an app change; everything else is delivered now. | tag: yes |
| D9 | **API key now, OAuth later.** An `Authenticator` interface with one implementation (API key plus a per-key policy row). OAuth 2.1 is specified in section 3.4 and gated on a TLS hostname. | `claude.ai` and ChatGPT need OAuth and HTTPS [R landscape 3.2]; Claude Code, Cursor, VS Code and stdio do not. | no |
| D10 | **Dual-era, stateless, SDK v2** (`@modelcontextprotocol/server` 2.3.1, exact pins). No SSE, no sessions, no subscriptions, no elicitation dependence. | Clients still speak the 2025 handshake; the 2026-07-28 revision is stateless. v2 serves both from one factory [W]. | no |
| D11 | **Projection on the way in, allowlists on the way out.** The loader COPIES only the keys an engine or a tool reads (an allowlist per table, `fields.ts projectDoc`): an unknown key, a field a newer app added and a field a REST write planted never get in. Output builders pick named fields (never spread a document). Payroll and employee data are never loaded; e-invoice records are loaded as `{ id, cid, source, voucherTypeId, number, docDate, status, credible }` only (no IRN, QR, buyer GSTIN or remark). A test classifies every key of **every** loaded `types.ts` interface, and a trace test fails when an engine reads a key the allowlist lacks. | A blocklist misses what nobody listed: `redactForRead` misses `vaultPin` and `pin` [R audit finding 9], and the first pass's deletion list kept the Company's address, e-mail and TAN, an e-invoice's signed QR and a cheque's instrument number. | no |
| D12 | **Money is a decimal string, a balance is `{amount, side}`, ids only.** No tool accepts a name where an id is needed except `books_find`. Write tools take `{id, name}` references and check the name. | `(1000)` is negative in this app and `5 L` is five litres: no magnitude shorthand, no parsing of display text by the model. | no |
| D13 | **Sixteen tools: 14 read, 2 write.** Prefix `books_` (brand-independent: the product name still has to clear a trademark search). A read-only key lists 14. **Ledger creation is not in v1:** a ledger has no draft state and no undo, so it would be the one write a person could not simply decline; its design stays in 2.4.6 for v1.1. | Outcome tools, not one per endpoint [R use-cases 6.1]; the one write with no draft is deferred until the app has a place to hold it (review round, Appendix F). | no |
| D14 | **Kill switches and gates, all default to "off":** env `MCP_ENABLED` (the whole plugin); env `MCP_WRITES_ENABLED` (write tools and write routes); a per-tenant row that **Comhard creates** (`mcp_tenant.allowed`, `writes_allowed`: only owner-pool SQL or an operator tool sets them; **an absent row means disabled**, so setting the process flag turns nothing on for any customer); the tenant's own `enabled` and `writes_enabled` (an owner or admin can only narrow what Comhard allowed); per-key approval (**a key with no approved policy row cannot use MCP**, D22); per-key revoke and per-key `disabled`. | The plugin ships dormant, and the process flag is not a rollout switch: stage 1 is one tenant, not every tenant. | no |
| D15 | **No remote customer traffic over plain HTTP, enforced by the server as well as the client.** The server refuses every data route unless the TCP peer is loopback, or a declared TLS-terminating proxy vouches for https (`MCP_REQUIRE_SECURE`, default on, 1.6 item 6); the stdio client also refuses a non-loopback `http://` base URL unless an explicit flag is set. Real customer keys wait for TLS. | A key and a whole book in clear text [R landscape 4, audit finding 13]; the stdio check alone is bypassed by every client that sends `--header "X-API-Key: ..."` to the remote endpoint. | timing: yes |
| D16 | **MCP writes fire no webhooks.** They do nudge the SSE stream so devices pull in about a second. | A draft is not a business event until a person promotes it; app edits fire none either [R audit finding 11]. | yes |
| D17 | **Reads follow REST after a trial ends (still open); writes need `canSync`.** | Matches `rest.js:25-26` and "data is never stranded". | yes |
| D18 | **One bundled, exact-pinned file; the stdio server refuses non-loopback plain HTTP; no URL or key is baked in; the key comes only from the environment, never an argument.** Package name `@comhard/books-mcp`, **unpublished** until the owner decides (section 5, question 11). | A floating tag or a default URL are how a changed description or a clear-text key reach a customer; a process list shows arguments to every user. | name and publishing: yes |
| D19 | **The output allowlist is a strict schema**, enforced in every test and in production (a result that fails it becomes `INTERNAL_ERROR`), and **not advertised** in `tools/list`. | An output with a field nobody allowed is exactly a data-leak bug and should fail closed; advertising 14 output schemas costs a model about 60 KB of context on every session [J]. | no |
| D20 | **Compute never runs on the sync server's event loop, and the bundle is hash-pinned.** In the plugin, every load's post-processing and every engine runs in a `worker_threads` worker with a heap limit, a per-call deadline and one call at a time per tenant; the plugin refuses to load a bundle whose SHA-256 is not the pinned one (`MCP_BUNDLE_SHA256`, `MCP_WORKER_SHA256`). A worker thread is a **denial-of-service control, not a security boundary**: before the first customer key (stage 3) the reads run in a **separate process that holds no database credential and no secret** (`httpMain` over loopback, slice R-iso) unless the owner decides otherwise. | One tenant's GST call on a large book must not stall every device's sync, and a poisoned dependency inside the process that holds `JWT_SECRET` and the owner database URL [C config.js:17-27] is a whole-platform compromise, not an MCP bug. | yes (question 12) |
| D21 | **The write tier has ceilings that do not depend on a person noticing.** Defaults: voucher kinds `receipt,payment,contra,journal` only (the plugin refuses to start with writes on and `MCP_WRITE_KINDS` unset); at most 25 unpromoted AI drafts per company (`OPEN_DRAFT_LIMIT`); at most 10 drafts an hour, 50 a day and ₹1 crore of draft value a day per key, **counted from `mcp_commit`** so a restart does not reset them; no TDS or TCS entry; no ledger creation. | The token is not an approval (D5) and clients that auto-approve are common [R landscape 4.3]; the worst case of a fully obedient injected model must be a small, visible, unpromoted queue. | yes (questions 2, 3) |
| D22 | **MCP keys are read-scope keys, approved by an owner or admin; MCP writes never ride the REST `write` scope.** The MCP routes accept only a key whose scopes are exactly `read` **and** which has an approved `mcp_key_policy` row (`approved_by`); a key with the `write` scope is refused (`KEY_TOO_BROAD`) because it is already a full-tenant REST write key that no MCP rule can bind. Write ability is `policy.writes`, set by an owner or admin. | `/v1` reads no policy [C rest.js:31, platform.js:21-30]: a key that can create AI drafts through MCP must not also be able to `POST` and `DELETE` vouchers through REST. Any signed-in member can mint a key [C platform.js:81], so minting cannot be the approval. | no |

**Veto rows and their questions in section 8:** D4 is question 2; D8's tag is question 4; D15's timing is question 1; D16 is question 9; D17 is question 8; D18's name and publishing are question 11; D20 is question 12; D21's defaults are questions 2 and 3; D22 is question 6 (its default is not a veto row because the alternative leaves a leaked key able to write through REST). Questions 5, 7, 10 and 13 to 17 are about switches, machines, law and customers rather than a numbered decision.

### 0.2 The owner's side question: are the desktop and mobile apps built?

Verified in the repo today [C]:

* **Desktop (Electron): built, beta, not distributed.** Electron 31 shell in `electron/`; `electron-builder` targets are `dmg` and `zip` for macOS and `nsis` for Windows [C package.json "build"]. `release/Comsky-Books-Setup-0.1.0-x64.exe` (83,983,844 bytes) was built on 2026-10-19. It is unsigned, has never been run on a Windows machine, and has no download page [C COMSKY_BOOKS_PRODUCT.md:354]. **macOS is planned, not built:** `release/` holds only two `.blockmap` files from 2026-08-30 builds under the old name, no current `.dmg` [C COMSKY_BOOKS_PRODUCT.md:355, `ls release/`]. It does run on a Mac in development (`npm run electron:dev`).
* **Mobile: not developed.** No iOS or Android project, no React Native, no web manifest, no service worker (grep of `src`, `index.html`, `package.json`, `vite.config.ts` finds none) [C COMSKY_BOOKS_PRODUCT.md:352-353, 423]. `docs/ROADMAP.md:32` lists "Mobile apps (iOS/Android)" as an XL, unstarted epic. A phone can open the web app in its browser; usability on a phone is unverified.
* **Why it matters here:** a desktop-only customer who never signs in to the cloud has no tenant data, so a remote MCP sees nothing for them. Their route is the backup-file mode (D1, section 5.4). And because there is no mobile app, the OAuth connector (section 3.4) is how an owner would ask their books a question from a phone through Claude's or ChatGPT's own app [R landscape 3.3].

### 0.3 Where this spec departs from the research, and why

| Research said | This spec | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Writes go to an "AI Inbox" in the app that a person accepts (use-cases 3.4) | Preview then confirm, saving an Optional voucher | The task fixes preview/confirm. The Inbox is the better v2: it needs an app screen and a store, so it is listed (section 2.6) not built. Optional vouchers are the Inbox that already exists. |
| Stdio computes locally from `/v1` (landscape 7.1) vs stdio is a thin proxy (use-cases 6.6) | Both, per capability: reads run locally on a snapshot, writes always run on the server | Reads need no seam and work against today's deployed backend; writes cannot be trusted to a local process. |
| Seams R1-R3, W1-W4, K1-K2 need edits to `rest.js`, `sync.js`, `platform.js` | **All of them are delivered as new routes and tables inside `backend/src/mcpRoute.js`** | A new file mounted behind one line edits nothing that exists (section 1.6; the one touch of an existing table is a unique index on `api_key`, which changes no column and no row). The gaps in the existing REST routes (delete ghost, unredacted `vaultPin`) stay open and are listed in 7.5 as owner decisions, not dependencies. |
| `books_triage_bank_lines` in the launch set | Deferred to v1.1 | It takes a block of text written by outsiders (statement lines) and needs the bank-statement parsers; section 2.6. |
| Master creation comes after drafts (audit 3.7 item 4); the first pass of this spec still shipped `books_*_ledger` in v1 | **Ledger creation is not in v1** | A ledger has no draft state and no undo, a lookalike supplier is a payment-fraud set-up, and the app has no queue to hold it; the design stays in 2.4.6 for v1.1 behind its own per-key flag. (Review round.) |
| An MCP write key carries the REST `write` scope (first pass; the audit's K1) | MCP keys are read-scope; write ability is an approved policy flag (D22) | The REST scope would make every drafting key a full REST write key. (Review round.) |

## 1. Architecture

### 1.1 Shape

```
                     customer's AI client (Claude Desktop / Code, Cursor, VS Code ...)
                                 |  stdio (JSON-RPC lines)                    |  Streamable HTTP (POST /mcp)
                                 v                                            v
 +--------------------------------------------+        +----------------------------------------------+
 | mcp/ dist/stdio.mjs  (core lane)           |        | backend process  (remote lane)               |
 |   tools (read)  <- Snapshot cache          |        |   backend/src/mcpRoute.js  (flag, OFF)       |
 |   RestDataSource --HTTPS--> /mcp/gw/*  ----+--------+-> gateway routes (head, snapshot, changes,    |
 |        or /v1/* (read-only fallback)       | X-API- |      write/preview, write/commit)            |
 |   HttpWriteGateway (preview, commit)       | Key    |   /mcp (Streamable HTTP, stateless, dual-era)|
 |   FileDataSource (--backup file, read-only)|        |   tools (read)  <- Snapshot cache            |
 +--------------------------------------------+        |   PgDataSource -> change_log (RLS, tenant)   |
                                                       |   InProcessWriteGateway -> reserveNumber +   |
 engines: src/core/* bundled by esbuild into both     |      INSERT in ONE tenant transaction         |
 bundles (no src/ui, src/state, Dexie, React)          +----------------------------------------------+
```

* **Three entries, one library.** `mcp/src/stdio.ts` (local, API key from the environment), `mcp/src/httpMain.ts` (a standalone Streamable HTTP **pass-through** service: it holds no key and no database credential, forwards each caller's own `X-API-Key` to the backend gateway, and binds loopback only; it is the tests' server and the stage-3 isolation option of D20), and `mcp/src/http.ts` (the handler factory the backend plugin mounts). `mcp/src/host/worker.ts` is the compute worker the plugin starts (D20). Each is bundled by esbuild into one self-contained file under `mcp/dist/` (`stdio.mjs`, `http.mjs`, `httpMain.mjs`, `worker.mjs`).
* **The backend plugin.** `backend/src/mcpRoute.js` exports `mcpPlugin`, a Fastify plugin. It does nothing unless `MCP_ENABLED=1`. It loads `mcp/dist/http.mjs` with a dynamic `import()` (default path `../../mcp/dist/http.mjs` from `backend/src`, which is `/home/ubuntu/comhard/mcp/dist/http.mjs` on the VM; override with `MCP_BUNDLE`), so with the flag off the backend does not even read the bundle, needs no new npm dependency and `backend/package.json` is untouched. **With the flag on, the plugin reads the file, compares its SHA-256 with `MCP_BUNDLE_SHA256` (and `worker.mjs` with `MCP_WORKER_SHA256`; both are required, and a mismatch or a missing value fails registration closed) and loads those verified bytes, not the path a second time** (so the file cannot change between the check and the load): the builder proves on Node 22 one of two routes, `import()` of a `data:text/javascript;base64,...` URL (the bundle is self-contained and uses no `import.meta`), or a copy written into a freshly created 0700 directory; the worker is started from the same verified bytes. The release script prints both hashes. This pins the code the process runs; it is not a sandbox (D20, 5.1).
* **The registration seam (the only edit outside `mcp/` and the new file)**, applied later by the orchestrator, one line in `backend/src/server.js` directly after line 51 (`await app.register(restRoutes);`) and before the SPA static block:
  ```js
  await app.register((await import('./mcpRoute.js')).mcpPlugin); // Comsky MCP: dormant unless MCP_ENABLED=1
  ```
* **Versions, pinned exactly** (npm registry on 2026-10-22 [R landscape 2.1, re-checked with `npm view`]): `@modelcontextprotocol/server` 2.3.1, `@modelcontextprotocol/client` 2.3.1 (tests only), `@modelcontextprotocol/node` 2.1.1, `zod` 4.6.7 (import `* as z from 'zod/v4'` [W tools.md]), `esbuild` 0.28.2, `typescript` 5.9.3 (the root's installed version [C node_modules/typescript]; the registry's latest is 7.0.2, which this repo has not adopted: do not take it in `mcp/`), `@anthropic-ai/mcpb` 2.1.2 (dev only), `fastify` 5.x (dev only, for the in-process plugin test; exact version recorded from `npm view` on the day and matched to `backend/package.json`'s `^5.2.0`; `backend/node_modules` is **not installed** in this checkout, so the remote lane tests the plugin without importing anything from `backend/`'s dependency tree) (all of the above re-confirmed with `npm view` on 2026-10-22: server 2.3.1, node 2.1.1, client 2.3.1, zod 4.6.7, esbuild 0.28.2, mcpb 2.1.2, `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` still 1.32.1 as the maintenance line). `@modelcontextprotocol/fastify` is **not** used: the plugin lives inside the existing Fastify app, so it performs its own Host and Origin checks (section 1.6). Node >= 22 (the VM runs 22 [C docs/HANDOFF.md:57]); the `.mcpb` runs on Claude Desktop's bundled Node, version unknown, so **no native modules and no postinstall** [R landscape 2.1]. `@types/node` (dev only; the Node 22 line, exact version recorded from `npm view` on the day) is the one more dev dependency `types: ["node"]` needs. The runtime bundle inputs are `@modelcontextprotocol/server`, its dependency `@modelcontextprotocol/core` (2.3.1), `@modelcontextprotocol/node` with its dependencies `hono` and `@hono/node-server`, `zod`, and the engines [W `npm view`, 2026-10-22: node 2.1.1 depends on `hono ^4.11.4` and `@hono/node-server ^1.19.9`; server 2.3.1 on `zod ^4.2.0` and `core 2.3.1`]; `bundle.mjs` writes the full input list of each bundle to `dist/<entry>.inputs.txt`, and a release diff of that list is part of the review.

### 1.2 What each deployment can do

| | A. stdio + `RestDataSource` | B. plugin in the backend | C. `httpMain` standalone | D. stdio + `--backup` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Reaches | Claude Desktop, Code, Cursor, VS Code | Claude Code, Cursor, VS Code with a header key; claude.ai and ChatGPT only after OAuth (3.4) | same as B | Claude Desktop, Code, Cursor |
| Reads | local snapshot from `/mcp/gw/*`; falls back to `/v1/*` against a backend without the plugin | in a compute worker of the backend process, direct SQL pages, incremental refresh | like A, on a server | local file |
| Writes | yes, through `/mcp/gw/write/*` when the plugin is on; **read-only against a plain backend** | yes (when `MCP_WRITES_ENABLED=1`, tenant row and key policy allow) | via gateway, as A | never |
| Needs deploy | nothing for read-only on small books (works against `a0fffd6` today, 1.5.4); the plugin deploy for writes and for customers' real books | backend rsync + bundle + env | a second process | nothing |
| Needs TLS | yes before any real key (D15) | yes | yes | no network |

[J] A is the first thing a customer can use and needs no backend change. B is what makes writes safe and fast and is what claude.ai/ChatGPT will eventually point at.

### 1.3 Repository layout (new folder `mcp/`; every file is new)

```
mcp/
  package.json  package-lock.json  tsconfig.json  README.md  CHANGELOG.md  manifest.json (.mcpb)  engine-lock.json  DEPLOY.md  .gitignore (dist, .cache)
  scripts/   bundle.mjs  test.mjs  gate.mjs  mutate.mjs  mutants.mjs  drift.mjs  fixtures.mjs  fixtures.ts  smoke-stdio.mjs  release.mjs
             pg-gate.mjs  remote-smoke.mjs  explain250k.mjs                                   (the last three: remote lane)
  src/
    engines/   index.ts (the explicit allowlist of engine imports)  mirrors.ts
    data/      types.ts (FROZEN CONTRACT)  fields.ts  load.ts  cache.ts  restSource.ts  fileSource.ts  pgSource.ts
    format/    money.ts  period.ts  basis.ts  render.ts  cursor.ts  clean.ts  mask.ts  issues.ts
    safety/    limits.ts  limiter.ts  audit.ts  errors.ts
    tools/     registry.ts  schemas.ts  descriptions.ts  outputs.ts  <one file per tool>.ts
    write/     types.ts (FROZEN CONTRACT)  intents.ts  buildVoucher.ts  (buildLedger.ts: v1.1, 2.4.6)  validate.ts  token.ts  ids.ts  inProcess.ts  httpGateway.ts
    prompts/   index.ts        resources/ index.ts
    server.ts  server.types.ts (FROZEN CONTRACT)  stdio.ts  http.ts  httpMain.ts  http/ (authenticate.ts  limits.ts  guards.ts  secure.ts  pgAudit.ts)
    host/      workerHost.ts  worker.ts  (the compute worker of D20; the plugin's PgDataSource stays on the main thread and feeds pages to it)
  test/        *.test.ts  fake-backend.ts  oracle/  contract/  corpus/  golden/  remote/  pg/
  eval/        launch20.json
backend/src/mcpRoute.js          (new, remote lane)
```
File ownership per lane is in section 7.3. `mcp/tsconfig.json` copies **every** `compilerOptions` key of the root `tsconfig.json` verbatim (`target ES2020`, `lib ["ES2020", "DOM", "DOM.Iterable"]`, `module ESNext`, `moduleResolution bundler`, `strict`, `noFallthroughCasesInSwitch`, `isolatedModules`, `skipLibCheck`, `noEmit` and the rest [C tsconfig.json]; a looser `lib` would hide the use of an API newer than the one the engines are written against) and adds only `types: ["node"]`, with `include: ["src", "test", "scripts/*.ts"]`; `tsc` follows the imports into `../src/core`, `../src/data/defaults.ts`, `../src/types.ts`. The root `tsconfig.json` includes only `src`, so `mcp/` is invisible to the root `tsc` [C tsconfig.json]; the root `scripts/assert/run.mjs` finds suites under `scripts/assert/` only. `mcp/` has its own gate (7.2) and **nothing under `mcp/` is run by, or added to, the root gate**.

### 1.4 The engines: bundled exactly like the perf harness

`scripts/perf/run.mjs:57-82` bundles the real engines with `esbuild.build({ bundle, format: 'esm', platform: 'node', metafile })` and then refuses to run if any input matches a forbidden pattern [C]. `mcp/scripts/bundle.mjs` does the same for every shipped entry and for the test bundles:

* **Forbidden in a shipped bundle** (checked against `metafile.inputs`, `absWorkingDir` set to the repo root; without it the paths are relative and every check passes on nothing [R audit App. B]): `node_modules/(react|react-dom|scheduler|dexie|zustand)/`, `src/state/`, `src/data/db.`, `src/data/` except `defaults.ts`, `src/ui/` (no exception: the perf harness allows `paletteRank.ts`, MCP does not need it), `src/sync/`, `src/keys.`, and a comment-stripped scan of every `src/` input for `localStorage`, `sessionStorage`, `indexedDB`, `document.`, `window.` (the name `window` as a *field* in `core/dashIndex.ts` is a known false positive: allowlist that one file), `navigator.`, `DOMParser`. The bundle script **asserts the scan found files** (the Phase 28 lesson: a check that scanned nothing passed for months).
* **One import door.** `mcp/src/engines/index.ts` re-exports exactly the engine functions the tools use, and nothing else in `mcp/src` imports from `../../src` directly (a lint in `drift.mjs` greps for it). The list: `closingBalances`, `periodTotals`, `postsToLedgers`, `voucherHeadlineAmount`, `netProfit` (`core/accounting.ts`); `ledgerBasis`, `basisRows`, `basisTotals`, `BASES` (`core/reportBasis.ts`); `plFigures`, `moveFromTotals`, `plStock` (`core/plFigures.ts`); `balanceSheetOf`, `SECRET_KEY` (`core/splitCompany.ts`); `outstandingBills`, `openBillsFor`, `ageingBucket`, `AGEING_BUCKETS` (`core/billwise.ts`); `ageingAsOn`, `addDays`, `addYears`, `fyStartFor`, `monthsBetween`, `monthStart`, `monthEnd` (`core/dates.ts`); `periodPresets`, `finYearOf`, `fyLabel` (`core/dashPeriod.ts`); `BRAND` (`src/brand.ts`, a constant with no imports); `classByGroup`, `buildDashIndex`, `balanceOf`, `ledgerBalances`, `cashFlowSeries`, `topLedgers`, `unreconciledCount`, `CASH_CLASSES` (`core/dashIndex.ts`) with `bucketsFor`; `rankParties`, `rankItems`, `monthlyTrend`, `stockAlerts`, `gstPayableCash`, `tdsPayable` (`core/dashboardData.ts`); `stockPositions`, `stockValueAsOn`, `stockItemRows`, `pendingOrders` (`core/inventory.ts`); `buildGstr3b`, `buildGstr1`, `gstSetOff`, `computeItemGst`, `computeLedgerGst`, `isIntraState`, `voucherGstSummary`, `partyGstIssues`, `splitByRate` (`core/gst*.ts`); `rule37` (`core/itc.ts`); `tdsSummary` (`core/tds.ts`), `tcsSummary` (`core/tcs.ts`); `checkIntegrity` (`core/integrity.ts`); `sealCancelled`, `stateLabel`, `leadOf`, `faceAmount` (`core/voucherCancel.ts`); `dayBookRows`, `registerRows`, `registerAmount`, `ledgerVoucherData`, `ledgerSplit` (`core/reportRows.ts`); `numberWindow`, `carriedNumber` (`core/voucherNumberFloor.ts`); `burnedNumber` (`core/einvoiceState.ts`); `reserveBody`, `numberKey`, `seriesOf` (`core/vchNumbering.ts`); `validateGstin`, `normalizeGstin`, `normalizePan`, `isValidPan`, `gstinMatchesState` (`core/gstin.ts`); `stateCodeOf`, `stateNameOf`, `GST_STATE_CODES` (`core/gstStates.ts`); `resolveItemGst`, `snapshotOf`, `itemFieldsOn` (`core/gstRates.ts`); `featuresOf`, `DEFAULT_FEATURES` (`types.ts`); `portalRecordProblem` (`core/einvoiceImport.ts`; the loader calls it on the full e-invoice record before projecting it, 1.5.2 step 2; its import closure `csv`, `einvoiceDoc`, `gst`, `gstin` is checked by the purity scan); `round2`; `gid`, `vtid`, `mainGodownId`, `defaultVoucherTypes` (`data/defaults.ts`); `VCH_BASES`, `baseInfo` (`types.ts`). The builder adds a name only with a test that uses it, and re-runs the import-closure scan (133 of 136 `core` modules are clean; the three that are not, `navPlan`, `snippetStore`, `undo`, are never imported [R audit 2.3]).
* **Mirrors (logic that still lives inside React or the store).** `mcp/src/engines/mirrors.ts` re-creates, each with a header comment naming its original and a drift guard in `mcp/scripts/drift.mjs` (the same idea as `scripts/perf/mirrors.mjs`: grep the original for the exact lines copied; fail when they move):

  | Mirror | Original | Used by |
  |---|---|---|
  | `nextVchNo`, `formatVchNo` (needs `numberWindow`, `carriedNumber`, `burnedNumber`) | `src/state/store.ts:719-753` | numbering floor, `numberStr` |
  | `partySideOf` over `classByGroup` | `src/ui/dash/widgets/compute/common.ts` (the function after "which side a bill is on") | outstandings, ledger kind |
  | the receivables/payables segmentation (headline, current, overdue, unapplied) | `src/ui/dash/widgets/compute/receivables.ts` `computeAr` | `books_outstandings` |
  | Round Off lookup by name, GST accounting-invoice entry order | `src/ui/VoucherScreen.tsx:2540-2780` (`save`) | invoice builder |
  | `fyStartMonthOf` (month of `finYearFrom`, April when unknown) | `src/ui/dash/widgets/env.ts:53-56` | the bucket plan of `books_cash_position` only. **Not stock costing**: the Dashboard's stock pass and `balanceSheetOf` call the stock engine with no options (April) [C env.ts:85-92, splitCompany.ts:229], and the tools do the same so the identities of 6.3 hold (2.3 R9) |
  | `burnedNumberOf` over the **projected** e-invoice rows (`credible` instead of `portalRecordProblem(r)`) | `src/core/einvoiceState.ts:310-322` | the numbering floor of the write path; a test proves it equals `burnedNumber` on the full records of F0 and F2 |

  `computeAr` and the other Dashboard v2 compute modules are the **oracle** in the tests (6.3), not part of the shipped bundle: they are under `src/ui/` and so forbidden there, but a *test* bundle may import them (they import only types and `core/*`; the builder verifies this before relying on it, and falls back to engine-level identities if not).
* **Engine commit.** `bundle.mjs` stamps `git rev-parse --short HEAD` plus a dirty flag into the bundle (`__ENGINE_COMMIT__`), and every result prints it in `basis.engine`. A customer reporting a wrong figure names the commit, and `tools/list` changes only on a deliberate release.

### 1.5 The data layer

#### 1.5.1 The contract (frozen at slice C0; both lanes code against it)

```ts
// mcp/src/data/types.ts  (the architect's text: the C0 builder copies it verbatim and may only add, never change)
export type TableName =
  | 'groups' | 'ledgers' | 'vouchers' | 'voucherTypes' | 'units' | 'stockGroups' | 'stockCategories' | 'godowns'
  | 'stockItems' | 'tdsNatures' | 'costCategories' | 'costCentres' | 'einvoices';           // 13 tables + the company row
export const LOAD_TABLES: readonly TableName[] = [ /* the 13 above, this order */ ];
// NEVER loaded: employees, employeeGroups, payHeads, attendanceTypes, users, roles, customFields, leads, leadActivities,
// leaveTypes, leaveApplications, priceLevels, budgets, companies' security fields. (Privacy, 4.4; payroll lines are stripped from vouchers.)

export type RawDoc = Record<string, unknown>;
export interface RawBooks {
  cid: string;
  company: RawDoc | null;                         // the company row (may be redacted by the source)
  tables: Record<TableName, RawDoc[]>;            // latest non-deleted revision of every document of this company
  seq: number | null;                             // change-log revision the read reflects (null: unknown, v1 REST / file)
  readAt: number;                                 // epoch ms
}
export interface ChangeRow { seq: number; table: string; op: 'put' | 'delete'; id: string; doc?: RawDoc }

export type Scope = 'read' | 'write';
export interface KeyPolicy {
  approvedBy: string | null;                      // app_user.id of the owner or admin who approved this key; null = NOT APPROVED, the key cannot use MCP (D22). 'local' in stdio and file mode
  companyIds: readonly string[] | null;           // null = every company of the tenant, and the approver chose "all" EXPLICITLY (3.2)
  writes: boolean;                                // key may list and use the write tools (still needs the process, tenant and kind switches, 3.2)
  pii: 'masked' | 'full';                         // default 'masked'; governs ONLY what the server-side /mcp tools print (3.2): the gateway and stdio carry the full GSTIN and PAN the engines read
  expiresAt: number | null;                       // epoch ms
  disabled: boolean;
  limits: { readsPerMin: number; writesPerHour: number; writesPerDay: number; maxVoucherAmount: number | null;
            maxValuePerDay: number | null; maxOpenDrafts: number | null };   // null = the process default of Appendix C; a policy can only LOWER a default
}
export interface Principal {
  tenantId: string;                               // 'local' for stdio and file
  keyId: string;                                  // api_key.id, or 'local'
  keyName: string;                                // api_key.name, already cleaned (format/clean.ts)
  keyCreatedBy: string | null;                    // api_key.created_by: becomes change_log.user_id on a write
  scopes: readonly Scope[];                       // the MCP routes accept exactly ['read'] (D22); a key with 'write' is KEY_TOO_BROAD; stdio and file mode: ['read']
  policy: KeyPolicy;
  via: 'api-key' | 'oauth' | 'local';
}
export interface CompanyRow { id: string; name: string; finYearFrom: string; booksFrom: string; state: string; gstRegType: string | null;
  gstin: string | null; periodicity: 'Monthly' | 'Quarterly' | null; features: Record<string, boolean>; lockedThrough: string | null | 'unknown'; }

export interface DataSource {
  readonly kind: 'pg' | 'rest-gateway' | 'rest-v1' | 'backup-file';
  /** The companies this principal may see (the policy's company list already applied). */
  listCompanies(p: Principal, signal: AbortSignal): Promise<CompanyRow[]>;
  /** One cheap number: the tenant's change-log high-water mark (null when the source has none). */
  head(p: Principal, signal: AbortSignal): Promise<{ seq: number | null }>;
  /** A full, consistent read as of one seq (paged underneath, one `upto` for all pages). Refuses above `caps`. */
  load(p: Principal, cid: string, caps: LoadCaps, signal: AbortSignal): Promise<RawBooks>;
  /** Rows after `since` up to the current head, or 'too-many' (the caller then does a full load). Absent on sources that cannot. */
  changes?(p: Principal, cid: string, since: number, caps: { maxRows: number }, signal: AbortSignal): Promise<{ upto: number; rows: ChangeRow[] } | 'too-many'>;
  /** Null for read-only sources (backup file, REST against a plain backend). */
  readonly writes: WriteGateway | null;
}
export interface LoadCaps { maxVouchers: number; maxBytesPerTable: number; maxDocBytes: number; maxDocsPerTable: number }   // a breach aborts the load at the first page that exceeds it (BOOK_TOO_LARGE); an over-size single document is quarantined instead
// WriteGateway, PreviewRequest, PreviewResult, CommitRequest, CommitResult: mcp/src/write/types.ts (section 2.4.5)
```

#### 1.5.2 Turning documents into engine inputs, headlessly (`data/load.ts`)

The app's recipe is `store.ts loadInto(cid)` [C store.ts:306-362]. The loader does the same minus every write, in this order. Each step has a test.

1. **Company filter.** Keep a document only if `doc.cid === cid` (the company row: `doc.id === cid`). `GET /v1/:collection` without `?cid=` mixes every company [R audit 1.3]; this filter is applied even when the source already filtered.
2. **Project** (`data/fields.ts projectDoc(table, doc)`, run where the full document first arrives: in the backend for the gateway and `pg` sources, in the client for `rest-v1` and backup files, so the gateway never ships a field the tools do not need). The projection **copies** the allowlisted keys by explicit property reads (never `Object.assign`, spread or `delete` on an input document, so a `__proto__` or `constructor` key in hostile JSON cannot reach a prototype); everything else is absent from the snapshot, whether it is a secret, a personal detail, a newer app's field or a value a REST write planted. A document whose JSON exceeds `maxDocBytes` (256 KiB, measured before projection) is quarantined, not loaded. **The allowlists are evidence-based, not hand-listed.** `fields.ts` holds, per loaded table, `keep` (a one-line reason each) and `drop` (with its reason), and three tests hold them: (a) the **classification test** parses every loaded interface of `src/types.ts` (`Company`, `Group`, `Ledger`, `Unit`, `StockGroup`, `StockCategory`, `Godown`, `StockItem`, `VoucherType`, `CostCategory`, `CostCentre`, `TdsNature`, `VoucherEntry`, `InventoryLine`, `Voucher`, `EinvoiceRecord`; a line-oriented regex over `^\s{2}(\w+)\??:`, the blunt method `scripts/assert/master-resave.assert.ts` uses) and **fails on any key with no classification**, so a new field forces a decision; (b) the **trace test** runs all 14 read tools and the write planner over F0, F2 and F2c with every projected document wrapped in a recording `Proxy`: a key that is read but not `keep` fails (the engines would silently see `undefined`), and a `keep` key that is never read is listed in the test output as a candidate to drop; (c) the **never list** below can never be moved to `keep` (the test asserts the intersection is empty; a trace that wants one of them goes to the architect, not to the builder). **Never kept, whatever an engine might read:** `Company.vault, vaultLegacy, vaultPin, security` (the period lock `security.lockedThrough` is lifted into snapshot meta first when the source can read it), `logo, ui, dashboard, reportViews, print, voucherView, mailingName, address, pincode, city, email, phone, phone2, pan, tan, deductorName, cin, udyam, iec, website, jurisdiction, tdsSeedVersion`; `Ledger.address, contact, phone, email, favouringName, bankAcNo, bankIfsc, bankName, bankBranch, bankHolder, bankBsr, chequeLayout, chequeBooks, stmtFormat, openingBankBalance, udf` (`gstin` and `pan` stay: the GST and TDS engines read them; the `/mcp` tools mask them on output, which does not bind the gateway: 3.2); `StockItem.udf, bom, prices`; `Voucher.payroll` (set to `[]`), `dispatch, ewb, exportInfo`; `VoucherEntry.instrument, instrumentDate, instrumentType, chequeFavouring, chequeCrossing, chequePrint, challan` (a cheque or UTR number is a bank detail; `bankDate`, which the unreconciled count reads, and `tdsNatureId` stay); and on every table every key `SECRET_KEY` matches (`core/splitCompany.ts:192`: `pin`, `pinhash`, `vault*`, `security`, `recoverykey`, anything ending in `secret`, `password`, `passcode`, `api_key`, `token`, `credential`). **E-invoice records** are projected to `{ id, cid, source, voucherTypeId, number, docDate, status, credible }`, where `credible` is `!portalRecordProblem(record)` computed on the FULL record first: the numbering floor skips a portal row that fails that test, so the projection must carry its verdict, because the IRN, Ack number and GSTIN the test reads are never loaded; the IRN, signed QR, buyer GSTIN, e-way details and cancel remark never leave the database.
3. **Quarantine** (`shapeProblem(table, doc): string | null`). A document the engines would throw on, or silently corrupt a total with, is *excluded and reported*, never fed in: a voucher with a `base` not in `VCH_BASES` makes `closingBalances` throw `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'accounting')`; no `entries` array on an accounting base throws `v.entries is not iterable`; a string `amount` of `"5"` turns a balance into the string `"05"` [R audit 3.1, probed]. Voucher rules: `id`, `typeId` strings; `base` in `VCH_BASES`; `date` a real `YYYY-MM-DD`; `number` a finite number; `entries` an array (may be `[]` only for a non-accounting base or a cancelled shell); every entry has a string `ledgerId`, `type` exactly `'Dr'|'Cr'`, `amount` `typeof 'number'` and finite; `inventory`, when present, an array whose lines have finite `qty` and `amount`. Ledger: `groupId` string, `openingBalance` finite number, `openingType` `'Dr'|'Cr'`. Stock item: `unitId` string, finite openings. Quarantined documents are listed (id, table, reason, at most 50) in snapshot meta, counted in `basis.data_notes`, and are the subject of the `books_run_checks` `integrity` set. An entry pointing at a ledger that no longer exists is tolerated (the engines skip it; `closingBalances` adds a stray map key) and counted as a data note. **Hostile documents:** a document whose `id` is `__proto__`, `constructor` or `prototype`, or whose JSON nests deeper than 16 levels, is quarantined ("hostile document"); every id index in the snapshot (`ledgersById`, the incremental refresh's replace-and-delete by id, the cursor tables) is a `Map`, never a plain object.
4. **Seal and sort exactly as the store does** [C store.ts:294-295, perf `passes.ts:200-201`]: `vouchers.map(sealCancelled)` (a half-cancelled voucher an older client wrote reads as live without it, and the GST, stock and order engines test `v.optional` directly, not `postsToLedgers` [C voucherCancel.ts:222-238]), after first ordering by `id` in UTF-16 code-unit order, then a **stable** sort by `(date, number)`. The `id` pre-order reproduces Dexie's primary-key order for same-date, same-number vouchers, which FIFO and average-cost valuation depend on (`stockMovements` orders same-date movements by array position [C inventory.ts:33-48]).
5. **In-memory seeds, writing nothing.** For every `VCH_BASES` entry whose `vtid(cid, base)` is absent from `voucherTypes`, add the entry from `defaultVoucherTypes(cid)` to the in-memory array (the app writes these on open [C store.ts:321-350]; a tenant whose devices never reopened the company has none in the change log). Nothing is persisted.
6. **Derived, memoised per snapshot version:** `features = featuresOf(company)` (an absent `Company.features` reads as the app reads it, `DEFAULT_FEATURES` with bill-wise on, not as `undefined`: `computeAr` takes its not-billwise branch when `feats.billwise` is not `true`, so reading the raw field would print no ageing for a company the app ages), `ledgersById`, `itemsById`, `typesById`, `groupClass = classByGroup(groups, cid)`, `partySideOf(ledgerId)`, `groupPath(groupId)` (names root to leaf), and the statistics block: counts per base, `optional`, `cancelled`, `postDated`, `nonPosting` (`!baseInfo(base).accounting`), `afterToday`, `minDate`, `maxDate`, `quarantined`.
7. **"Today" is a parameter.** The engines never read the clock except the user-typed date mnemonic `t` in `dates.parseDateInput` [C dates.ts:108], which no tool calls. The tools get `today` from `format/period.ts todayIST(now)` = `new Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-CA', { timeZone: 'Asia/Kolkata', year: 'numeric', month: '2-digit', day: '2-digit' }).format(now)` and pass it to `ageingAsOn(asOn, today)` and `buildDashIndex`. `core/dates.ts toISO` uses *local* getters, so on a UTC host `toISO(new Date())` is the wrong date for 5.5 hours of every Indian day [R audit 2.4(6)]. The process TZ is **not** changed (it would change the whole backend). `now` is injectable so tests pin the clock.
8. **Period.** `from`/`to` per call. Presets are resolved by `core/dashPeriod.ts periodPresets(today, fy, booksFrom)` with `fy` = the company's financial year containing `today` (`finYearFrom` rolled forward by whole years until it contains `today`; before `finYearFrom` it is the first year). The default period is the whole of that financial year (**except** `books_gst_summary`, which defaults to the latest complete return period: 2.3 R10). The app itself opens a company at `finYearFrom` to one year later and never rolls it forward [C store.ts:351-352], so for a company whose year was not rolled in the app the tools' default year (the one containing today) differs from the screen's opening period; `basis.data_notes` then says "the app opens this company at FY 2025-26; this result uses FY 2026-27, the year containing today". **Preset gaps are filled, not surfaced as errors.** `periodPresets` (ids `thisMonth`, `lastMonth`, `thisQuarter`, `lastQuarter`, `thisYear`, `lastYear`) clamps to the open financial year and *omits* a preset that would start before it: there is no `lastMonth` in the first month of the year, no `lastQuarter` in the first quarter, and no `lastYear` when the books begin after it ends [C core/dashPeriod.ts:43-70 header and body, read 2026-10-22]. A person asking "last month" on 5 April must still get March, so the mapping is `this_month` to `thisMonth`, `this_quarter` to `thisQuarter`, `this_fy` to `thisYear`, and for `last_month`, `last_quarter`, `last_fy` the preset when it exists, otherwise the same calendar window computed unclamped with `monthStart`, `monthEnd` and `addYears`/`finYearOf` from `core/dates.ts` and `core/dashPeriod.ts`, with a `data_notes` line "this period starts before the open financial year" (a window that precedes `booksFrom` is `INVALID_PERIOD`). `fy_to_date` is `{ from: fy.from, to: min(today, fy.to) }`. A fiscal quarter follows the company's own year start. A test pins `last_month` on 5 April, `last_quarter` on 10 May, and `last_fy` in the first year of the books. April is hard-wired as the FY month in `fyStartFor` and in valuation [C dates.ts:50, R audit 2.4(5)]; for a company whose `finYearFrom` month is not April the basis block says "voucher-number windows and stock costing restart assume April to March (an app limitation)".

**The guard every total must respect** (the sentence from `CLAUDE.md`, made checkable): a figure that sums vouchers must exclude Optional, Cancelled and non-posting vouchers. The engines the tools call enforce it in three ways, and **the tool layer adds none of its own and removes none**: (A) ledger balances via `postsToLedgers(v) = baseInfo(v.base).accounting && !v.optional && !v.cancelled` inside `closingBalances`, `periodTotals`, `ledgerBasis`, `ledgerVoucherData`, `outstandingBills`, `tdsSummary`, `buildDashIndex` [C accounting.ts:9]; (B) stock through `v.optional` and `stock: 'none'` in `stockMovements`/`stockPositions` and Physical Stock resolved as a delta against the running per-godown balance [C inventory.ts:26-31, 113-125]; (C) GST through `!v.optional` in `buildGstr1`/`buildGstr3b` (sealed vouchers only). Lists that must show everything (the Day Book, `books_search_vouchers`) tag each row with `stateLabel(v)` and a default filter that shows Regular only. **Every figure-bearing result states what was left out** (`basis.excluded`). Section 6.4 proves it with a metamorphic suite (add poison vouchers, nothing moves) rather than by review.

#### 1.5.3 Snapshot cache (`data/cache.ts`)

* Key `tenantId|cid`. An entry holds the sealed arrays, the meta (above), `seq`, `loadedAt`, `checkedAt`, a monotonic `version`, and the memoised derived maps.
* **Freshness.** Serve from the entry while `now - checkedAt < ttl` (default **60 s**; **15 minutes** in `rest-v1` mode, 1.5.4). After that, call `source.head()` (16 ms at 294k rows [R audit 1.6]): unchanged seq extends `checkedAt`; changed seq goes to `source.changes(since)` and applies the rows incrementally (insert, replace, delete by id; re-run steps 2-6 on the changed documents only; re-sort the voucher array), or does a full `load` when the source has no `changes` or answers `'too-many'` (> 20,000 rows). `books_refresh` and every `books_confirm_*` force the check. Every result prints `books_as_of` (`seq`, `loaded_at`, `checked_at`, IST).
* **Single flight per key**; **one call at a time per tenant** in the compute worker (a tenant that alternates four companies queues behind itself, never behind another tenant); a **global load semaphore of 1** (a load holds a database connection; the backend pool is 10 and the owner pool is 2 [C db.js:8,10]); queue wait capped at 20 s, then `DATA_UNAVAILABLE`; a global compute queue of at most 8 waiting calls (beyond it `RATE_LIMITED`); a per-call deadline (`MCP_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`, default 30 s) after which the worker is terminated and restarted.
* **Caps** (config; defaults are for the VM until it is measured, the owner supplies its RAM: section 8, question 7): `maxVouchers` **100,000** per snapshot in the plugin (300,000 only after question 7's answer); `maxDocsPerTable` for every other table (ledgers and stock items 50,000, e-invoices as vouchers, the rest 20,000); `maxDocBytes` 256 KiB; `maxBytesPerTable` 128 MiB for **every** source (not only REST); `maxResident` 3 snapshots and **`maxResidentPerTenant` 1**, least recently used evicted; a resident-memory budget (`MCP_MAX_RESIDENT_MB`, default 1,024) measured as the running sum of loaded JSON bytes, evicting to fit and refusing a single book that exceeds it; idle eviction at 10 minutes. A **pre-count** (`count(DISTINCT doc_id)` of the company's vouchers, 1.5.4) runs before the first page, and a breach of any cap aborts the load at the first page that exceeds it: the answer is `BOOK_TOO_LARGE` with the count, never a partial book (a partial book gives wrong totals with confident wording). The transient cost to plan for is about 0.8-1 GB per 250,000 vouchers [R audit 1.6 [J]]; the worker's heap limit (`MCP_WORKER_HEAP_MB`, default 1,536) turns an overrun into a dead worker and a `DATA_UNAVAILABLE`, not a dead sync server.
* **Epoch.** A tenant kill (3.5) bumps `mcp_tenant.epoch`; a cache entry created under an older epoch is dropped on next use, and the kill route also clears the entries directly for immediate effect.
* **Cursor binding (keyset, not offset).** List cursors are `base64url(JSON{v:1, tool, argsHash, tenant, key, after, exp}) + '.' + HMAC`, where `after` is the sort key of the last row returned (for example `[date, number, id]`), the HMAC key is a subkey derived from `MCP_TOKEN_SECRET` (HKDF, label `cursor`; a random per-process secret in stdio), `tenant` and `key` are the caller's tenant id and key id (a key id is not a secret, so it is bound inside the payload, never used as the MAC key), and a cursor is valid for 15 minutes **for the same tool, arguments, tenant and key**. The next page is "the rows after `after`" in the snapshot as it stands, so a refresh that lands between two calls does not invalidate the cursor, and a row is neither repeated nor skipped except by a change inside the part already read (every page prints its own `books_as_of`, and `data_notes` says when the books changed since the first page). A tampered, foreign or expired cursor is `STALE_CURSOR: repeat the first call`. The snapshot `version` bumps only when a row of this company or of a loaded table changed; it keys the memoised derived maps, not the cursors.

#### 1.5.4 The sources

**`PgDataSource`** (`data/pgSource.ts`, remote lane; runs on the **main thread** and feeds pages to the compute worker, 1.6 item 7; SQL only, with the backend's functions *injected*: `withTenant`, `withTenantLock`, `reserveNumber`, `cleanReserve`, `redactForRead`, so the module imports nothing from `backend/` and is testable with a fake client). Every query runs inside `withTenant(tenantId, ...)` so RLS applies, and **also** carries `tenant_id = $1` explicitly: the explicit predicate lets the planner use `idx_change_doc (tenant_id, tbl, doc_id, seq)` as an equality prefix, which RLS alone does not [C sync.js:344-355 comment]. The tenant id comes **only** from the verified `Principal`, never from an argument.

```sql
-- load, one page (repeat with the last doc_id until fewer than $6 rows come back; every page uses the same $5 = upto)
SELECT DISTINCT ON (doc_id) doc_id, seq, op, payload
FROM change_log
WHERE tenant_id = $1 AND tbl = $2 AND (cid = $3 OR cid = '') AND doc_id > $4 AND seq <= $5
ORDER BY doc_id, seq DESC
LIMIT $6;                                  -- 1,000; ~3.3 ms a page measured at 294k rows [R audit 1.6 [M]]
-- head
SELECT COALESCE(MAX(seq), 0) AS seq FROM change_log WHERE tenant_id = $1;
-- changes
SELECT c.seq, c.tbl, c.op, c.doc_id, c.payload FROM change_log c
WHERE c.tenant_id = $1 AND c.seq > $2 AND c.seq <= $3 AND c.tbl = ANY($5)
  AND (c.cid = $4 OR (c.cid = '' AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM change_log p          -- a REST delete row has cid = '': admit it only for a document this company once held
        WHERE p.tenant_id = $1 AND p.tbl = c.tbl AND p.doc_id = c.doc_id AND p.cid = $4 AND p.seq < c.seq)))
ORDER BY c.seq LIMIT $6;                   -- 0.43 ms a page of 1,000 [R audit 1.6 [M]] for the simpler form; the EXISTS form is re-measured in the EXPLAIN gate
-- pre-count (once, before the first page; an upper bound, because it still counts documents that were later deleted)
SELECT count(DISTINCT doc_id) AS n FROM change_log WHERE tenant_id = $1 AND tbl = 'vouchers' AND cid = $2 AND seq <= $3;
```
`(cid = $3 OR cid = '')` is deliberate: the REST DELETE writes its delete row with `cid = ''` [C rest.js:85], so grouping by `(cid, doc_id)` as `GET /v1` does returns a ghost of every API-deleted document [R audit finding 10, reproduced]. Grouping by `doc_id` alone and admitting the `''` rows makes the delete the latest revision, as every device sees it. `upto` is read first (`head`) and applied to every page of every table, which is what makes the snapshot consistent across tables (the pushes are serialised per tenant, so everything at or below `MAX(seq)` is committed [C db.js:36-52, sync.js:344-348]). Each page is its own short transaction (a connection is held for milliseconds, not for the whole load). The remote lane **re-measures these three queries with `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` on a throwaway Postgres at 250,000 vouchers as an exit gate** (the audit's figures are from Postgres 14 on a laptop, the VM runs 16).
**A company-restricted key must not learn about other companies.** The load query admits `cid = ''` rows only so that a delete reads as the latest revision; the snapshot route sends only documents whose latest revision is a **put**, so another company's tombstone id is never sent. The `changes` query admits a `cid = ''` delete row only when the same document id has an earlier revision under the requested company (the `EXISTS` above, which uses `idx_change_doc`). What remains visible to any key of the tenant is the **tenant-wide `seq` counter** (`head`, `upto`): a key limited to company A can see that something else happened in the tenant, not what or where; 3.2 states it. The counter cannot be made per company without breaking the consistent-read property (`upto` must be a tenant-wide point in time).

**`RestDataSource`** (`data/restSource.ts`, core lane). Constructed with `{ baseUrl, apiKey, fetch, clientId }`. On first use it calls `GET {base}/mcp/gw/info`. **Gateway mode (`rest-gateway`: `head`, `snapshot` pages with `upto`, `changes`, write routes) only when the answer is HTTP 200 with `content-type: application/json` and a body whose `gateway` is exactly `1`.** A plain backend with no `APP_PREFIX` answers any unknown GET with the SPA's `index.html` and status 200 [C server.js:56-66], and so does the app URL with its path prefix pasted as the base, so a bare 200 proves nothing. A 401 or 403 carrying the plugin's JSON error body is the gateway refusing this key (`AUTH_FAILED`, `KEY_TOO_BROAD`, `KEY_NOT_APPROVED`: shown to the customer as such, not as "plain backend"); anything else (404, HTML, non-JSON) means a plain backend (`rest-v1`: `GET /v1/{table}?cid=` per table, no `seq`, no `changes`, full reload on TTL, read-only). The README says the base URL is the origin only, without the app's path. Rules for both: `redirect: 'error'` (an `X-API-Key` header must never follow a redirect to another host); the base URL comes only from configuration, **never from a tool argument**; the response body is read with a running byte count and aborted at `maxBytesPerTable`; a 402 on a write is mapped to `SUBSCRIPTION_READ_ONLY`, a 401 to `AUTH_FAILED` (key revoked or wrong), a 429 honours `Retry-After`; every request has a 20 s timeout and the tool's `AbortSignal`; the key is never logged. In `rest-v1` mode `lockedThrough` is unknown (the company's `security` field is stripped by the server) and the basis says so; and a document deleted **through the REST API** can still be listed (the ghost: the delete row is written with `cid = ''` but the list groups by `(cid, doc_id)` [C rest.js:39, 85]; deletes made in the app are not affected), so every `rest-v1` result carries the data note "documents removed through the REST API may still appear in this source; the gateway source does not have this limitation". Both are reasons the gateway is the supported source and `rest-v1` is the no-deploy fallback **for the owner's own tests and small books only**. `GET /v1/{table}` has no pagination, limit or limiter: for each call the server runs `DISTINCT ON` over the tenant's whole log for that table, builds the whole array in the process that serves every device's sync, and serialises it before a client could abort [C rest.js:35-44]; the client's byte cap stops the download, not that work. So in `rest-v1` mode the freshness TTL is **15 minutes**, a reload happens only on `books_refresh` (limited to one per 5 minutes, then `RATE_LIMITED`), the byte cap is 32 MiB per table (a breach is `BOOK_TOO_LARGE`), the tables are fetched at different moments so a cross-table inconsistency is possible (`data_notes` says so), and `--check` **fails** when the company row in the answer carries any key `SECRET_KEY` matches (`vaultPin`, `pin`: the server has not shipped seam R5, 7.5), because R5 is a hard prerequisite of any customer key.

**`FileDataSource`** (`data/fileSource.ts`, core lane). Reads one `tallyweb-backup-v2` JSON (the on-disk tag is kept forever [C CLAUDE.md]; `core/backupMigrate.ts prepareRestore` is pure and migrates older files). The path comes from the operator's command line or environment, never from a tool. A file larger than 512 MB is refused. It runs the same loader, so a hand-edited backup cannot make an engine throw. It is also the deterministic fixture source for most tests.

### 1.6 The backend plugin (`backend/src/mcpRoute.js`, remote lane)

`export const mcpPlugin` wraps `createMcpPlugin(realDeps)`; `createMcpPlugin(deps)` is what the tests call with fakes (`deps` = `ownerQ`, `withTenant`, `withTenantLock`, `getEntitlement`, `reserveNumber`, `cleanReserve`, `notifyTenant`, `verifyAccess`, `makeWorker` (a fake in tests), `now`, `randomBytes`). `realDeps()` imports `./db.js`, `./subscription.js`, `./numbering.js`, `./realtime.js`, `./tokens.js` lazily, only when the flag is on. The plugin:

1. **Flags, config and fail-closed rules** (env, Appendix C). `MCP_ENABLED !== '1'`: return immediately, register nothing, log nothing. With `MCP_ENABLED=1` registration **throws (the backend refuses to start)** unless all of these hold: `JWT_SECRET` is set and is not the dev default `'dev-insecure-change-me'` [C config.js:27] (the management routes trust a bearer signed with it: with the default, anyone can mint an owner token); `MCP_TOKEN_SECRET` is at least 32 bytes (base64 or hex), **required whenever the plugin is on** (it derives three subkeys by HKDF, one each for confirmation tokens, list cursors and the audit address salt, so even a read-only deployment has a server secret); `MCP_BUNDLE_SHA256` and `MCP_WORKER_SHA256` match the two files (1.1); `MCP_ALLOWED_HOSTS` is set unless `HOST` is a loopback address (item 6); and the bundle loads. `MCP_WRITES_ENABLED !== '1'`: the write tools and `/mcp/gw/write/*` do not exist (404, and `tools/list` omits them). If writes are enabled the plugin additionally fails closed at registration when **`MCP_WRITE_KINDS` is unset** (there is no built-in list to fall back on, D21), or when `backend/src/numbering.js` cannot be imported or the `voucher_series` table (migration `008`) does not exist: **the write tier depends on the wave-3 backend, which is untracked and not deployed today** [C `git status`: `?? backend/src/numbering.js`, `?? backend/sql/008_voucher_series.sql`; the VM runs `a0fffd6`], so on the VM `MCP_WRITES_ENABLED=1` refuses to start until wave 3 is committed and deployed (section 7.4). `mcpRoute.js` statically imports `./config.js` first (that file, not Node, loads `backend/.env` [C config.js:8-24]) and only then reads any `MCP_*` variable, so a flag set in `backend/.env` is seen. It imports nothing else statically, so with the flag off it costs one already-loaded module.
2. **Embedded, idempotent DDL** (run once at registration with the owner pool, only when the flag is on, so flag-off means zero database effect; the DDL may be moved verbatim into `backend/sql/009_mcp.sql` if the owner prefers the convention). The VM's `comhard_app` role already has default privileges for tables the owner creates [C docs/HANDOFF.md §2a]:
   ```sql
   -- the one touch of an existing table: a unique INDEX (no column, no data change) so a policy row can be tied to its key's tenant by a composite foreign key
   CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS uq_api_key_id_tenant ON api_key(id, tenant_id);
   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS mcp_tenant (
     tenant_id uuid PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES tenant(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
     allowed boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false,         -- COMHARD's switch: owner-pool SQL or an operator tool only, never a tenant route (an absent row = disabled)
     writes_allowed boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false,  -- COMHARD's switch for the write tier, same rule
     enabled boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true,          -- the tenant's own narrowing switch (PUT /mcp/gw/tenant, /mcp/gw/kill); effective = allowed AND enabled
     writes_enabled boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false,  -- the tenant's own; effective = writes_allowed AND writes_enabled
     epoch int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, updated_by uuid, updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now());
   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS mcp_key_policy (
     key_id uuid PRIMARY KEY, tenant_id uuid NOT NULL,
     approved_by uuid NOT NULL, approved_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),   -- no row, no MCP (D22)
     company_ids text[], writes boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false,
     pii text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'masked' CHECK (pii IN ('masked','full')),
     expires_at timestamptz, disabled boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false,
     limits jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb, updated_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
     FOREIGN KEY (key_id, tenant_id) REFERENCES api_key(id, tenant_id) ON DELETE CASCADE);
   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS mcp_commit (           -- the AUTHORITATIVE record of what the MCP wrote, and the replay record (2.4.5)
     tenant_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES tenant(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
     doc_id text NOT NULL, key_id uuid NOT NULL, cid text NOT NULL, kind text NOT NULL,
     payload_hash text NOT NULL, number_str text, seq bigint NOT NULL, amount numeric NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
     created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(), PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, doc_id));
   CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_mcp_commit_key_at ON mcp_commit(tenant_id, key_id, created_at DESC);
   CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_mcp_commit_cid_at ON mcp_commit(tenant_id, cid, created_at DESC);
   CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS mcp_audit (
     id bigint GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY PRIMARY KEY,
     tenant_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES tenant(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
     at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(), key_id uuid, key_name text, cid text, tool text NOT NULL,
     client text, args jsonb, result jsonb, ms int, ip_hash text, doc_ids text[]);
   CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_mcp_audit_tenant_at ON mcp_audit(tenant_id, at DESC);
   ALTER TABLE mcp_audit ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
   ALTER TABLE mcp_commit ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
   DO $$ BEGIN
     IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_policies WHERE schemaname = current_schema() AND tablename = 'mcp_audit' AND policyname = 'mcp_audit_isolation') THEN
       CREATE POLICY mcp_audit_isolation ON mcp_audit USING (tenant_id = current_setting('app.tenant_id', true)::uuid)
         WITH CHECK (tenant_id = current_setting('app.tenant_id', true)::uuid); END IF;
     IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_policies WHERE schemaname = current_schema() AND tablename = 'mcp_commit' AND policyname = 'mcp_commit_isolation') THEN
       CREATE POLICY mcp_commit_isolation ON mcp_commit USING (tenant_id = current_setting('app.tenant_id', true)::uuid)
         WITH CHECK (tenant_id = current_setting('app.tenant_id', true)::uuid); END IF;
     -- append-only for the runtime role: an audit row and a commit record are never updated or deleted by the app (removing a tenant cascades as the owner)
     IF EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 'comhard_app') THEN
       REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE ON mcp_audit, mcp_commit FROM comhard_app; END IF;
   END $$;
   ```
   `mcp_tenant` and `mcp_key_policy` are integration metadata looked up by key before a tenant context exists, so they are read on the owner pool, exactly like `api_key` [C 006_p4.sql header], **with explicit, aliased column lists and the tenant taken only from `api_key.tenant_id`** (item 3). `mcp_audit` and `mcp_commit` are tenant data: RLS plus `withTenant`, written on the app pool, **append-only for the runtime role**. The commit transaction (2.4.5) also re-reads `api_key`, `mcp_tenant` and `mcp_key_policy` through its own app-pool client (non-RLS tables the app role can read through the default privileges; the pg lane asserts it).
3. **Authentication** (`http/authenticate.ts`). Its own queries, not `resolveApiKey` (which discards the key id and name and reads no policy [C platform.js:9-16]).
   * **Pre-lookup guards (before any database call).** The header must match `^ck_[0-9a-f]{48}$`, the exact shape the key screen mints (`'ck_' + randomBytes(24).toString('hex')` [C platform.js:85]); anything else is `AUTH_FAILED` with no lookup. A **pre-auth token bucket** keyed on the TCP peer address (never `X-Forwarded-For`, which `trustProxy: true` lets a caller write [C server.js:29, ratelimit.js:42-45]; behind a declared TLS proxy the key is the right-most `X-Forwarded-For` entry, the one the proxy itself appended) plus one global ceiling (defaults: 30 failed or unknown-key requests a minute per address, 300 a minute overall) answers 429 before any lookup. At most **one** owner-pool lookup runs at a time, queued (the pool has two connections [C db.js:10], shared with `/v1`, roles and admin), and a per-minute count of failed authentications goes to the process log: not to a tenant table, because `mcp_audit.tenant_id` is NOT NULL and an unknown key has no tenant.
   * **The queries.** (i) `SELECT k.id AS key_id, k.tenant_id AS tenant_id, k.name AS key_name, k.scopes AS scopes, k.revoked AS revoked, k.created_by AS created_by FROM api_key k WHERE k.key_hash = $1` with `key_hash = sha256(key)`; the principal's tenant comes **only** from `k.tenant_id`. Then (ii) `SELECT p.approved_by, p.company_ids, p.writes, p.pii, p.expires_at, p.disabled, p.limits FROM mcp_key_policy p WHERE p.key_id = $1 AND p.tenant_id = $2`, and (iii) `SELECT t.allowed, t.writes_allowed, t.enabled, t.writes_enabled, t.epoch FROM mcp_tenant t WHERE t.tenant_id = $1`: every column named and aliased, **never `p.*` or `*`, and never one joined statement**. (The first pass joined `LEFT JOIN mcp_key_policy p ... SELECT ..., p.*`: both tables have a `tenant_id` (and `updated_at`), and node-postgres keeps the **last** of two same-named columns with no error [W `pg` 8.16.0 `lib/result.js` `parseRow` assigns `row[field]` in column order; the backend pins `^8.13.1`]. A key with no policy row would then resolve to `tenant_id = null`, and a "fix" by `COALESCE` would let a planted policy row re-point a key at another tenant.) The composite foreign key of item 2 makes a policy row for another tenant's key impossible, and a test plants one anyway (constraint dropped, owner SQL) and asserts the authenticator ignores it.
   * **The decision, in this order** (each failure is `AUTH_FAILED` with one message that does not say which, unless a code is named): no such key, or `revoked`; no `mcp_tenant` row, or `allowed` false, or `enabled` false (**an absent row means disabled**, D14); the key's scopes are not exactly `['read']` (**`KEY_TOO_BROAD`**, 403, which may be said to the holder of a valid key: a key with the `write` scope is already a full-tenant REST write key, D22); no policy row (**`KEY_NOT_APPROVED`**, 403: "ask an owner or administrator to approve this key for AI access"); `disabled`; `expires_at` in the past. Write ability exists only when the five switches of 3.2 hold.
   * **Cache.** The outcome (positive **and** negative) is cached 30 seconds per key hash in a bounded LRU (10,000 entries; a negative entry lives 5 seconds) with single flight; the cache holds the principal, never the raw key. `last_used_at` is updated at most once a minute per key. There is no per-call owner query; audit and commit rows are written on the app pool.
   Dashboard bearer tokens are **not** accepted on any MCP data route (the JWT has no audience [C tokens.js:7-9]); the management routes below are the only place a bearer is read.
4. **Principal carrier.** `AuthInfo` has `token, clientId, scopes, expiresAt, resource` (and the gate stamps `resourceMetadataUrl`); the docs do not say whether extra fields survive, only that a tool reads `ctx.http.authInfo` as the object the verifier returned [W authorization.md, 2026-10-22; "survives unchanged" is an inference]. The per-request factory passed to `createMcpHandler` receives `{ era, authInfo, requestInfo }` and is called once per request [W http.md]. Decision: the `Principal` rides in an `AsyncLocalStorage` set around the `handler.fetch(request, { authInfo })` call, because that depends on nothing undocumented (a test may later switch to an extra `authInfo` field if it proves to survive; the cross-check below stays either way); `authInfo.token` is an opaque non-secret constant (`'mcp'`), **never the API key** (the handler context is visible to every tool and to any log of it; a test searches every object reachable from a handler's context for the key and fails if it finds it; the SDK page does not say whether `token` is required [W http.md, 2026-10-22], so the builder sets the constant and checks the SDK accepts it); `authInfo.clientId` is set to `principal.keyId`, and every tool wrapper asserts `ctx.http?.authInfo?.clientId === principal.keyId` and fails closed on a mismatch. A test fires interleaved concurrent requests for two tenants and proves neither handler ever sees the other's principal (6.5).
5. **Routes** (all behind the flag; unknown paths under `/mcp/` answer 404 JSON through a catch-all so they never fall through to the SPA):
   * `POST /mcp`: the protocol endpoint. GET and DELETE answer 405 (the stateless dual-era handler does this [W legacy-clients.md]). Route `bodyLimit` 256 KiB (the backend's global limit is 20 MiB [C server.js:28]). Mount as the SDK documents for Fastify [W fastify.md]: `Object.assign(request.raw, { auth })`, `node(request.raw, reply.raw, request.body)`, with `toNodeHandler` from `@modelcontextprotocol/node` over `createMcpHandler`; because the reply is written on `reply.raw`, call `reply.hijack()` first so Fastify does not also try to send (the SDK page does not cover mounting into an existing Fastify instance [W], so the in-process Fastify test in 6.2 is the proof [J]). A hijacked reply runs none of Fastify's remaining hooks [W fastify.dev Reply.md], so the plugin sets `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and the content type itself on `reply.raw` before the handler runs; it ties an `AbortController` to `request.raw.on('close')` and a per-call deadline (`MCP_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`) that destroys the socket, so an exception or a stalled handler after the hijack cannot leave a socket open (a test throws after the hijack and asserts the connection closes; the server-wide `requestTimeout` is not changed).
   * `GET /mcp/gw/info`, `GET /mcp/gw/companies`, `GET /mcp/gw/head`, `GET /mcp/gw/snapshot`, `GET /mcp/gw/changes`, `POST /mcp/gw/write/preview`, `POST /mcp/gw/write/commit`: the gateway the stdio client uses (wire shapes in Appendix E). Same authentication, policy, limiter and audit as `/mcp`. Documents leave through `projectDoc` (the allowlist of 1.5.2, with `redactForRead` as a second net), so even the gateway never returns a field no tool needs.
   * Management, **Bearer JWT, owner or admin role only** (`membership.role in ('owner','admin')` for the token's own tenant [C 002_p2.sql]; the tenant is the token's `tid`, never a path or body value): `GET|PUT /mcp/gw/policy/:keyId`, `GET|PUT /mcp/gw/tenant`, `POST /mcp/gw/kill`, `GET /mcp/gw/audit?limit=`. **A key id in a path is never trusted for its tenant:** `GET` reads the policy through `api_key.id = $keyId AND api_key.tenant_id = $callerTenant`, and `PUT` writes `INSERT INTO mcp_key_policy (key_id, tenant_id, approved_by, ...) SELECT k.id, k.tenant_id, $user, ... FROM api_key k WHERE k.id = $1 AND k.tenant_id = $callerTenant ON CONFLICT (key_id) DO UPDATE ...`, so the stored `tenant_id` is the key's own and another tenant's key id matches no row and answers `NOT_FOUND` (the composite foreign key is the second net). **A `PUT` that creates a policy is an approval:** it must state `company_ids` explicitly (an array of the tenant's company ids, or the string `"all"`), and `approved_by` is the token's user id. A `PUT /mcp/gw/tenant` can set `enabled` and `writes_enabled` and nothing else (`allowed` and `writes_allowed` are Comhard's: owner-pool SQL only), and `writes_enabled: true` is refused unless `writes_allowed` is true. These are what an app "AI access" screen would call later; until then the owner uses `curl`.
6. **Secure transport, Host and Origin guards** (`http/secure.ts`, `http/guards.ts`; applied in this order to every `/mcp` route, data and management alike, before authentication; the handler validates none of this itself [W http.md]).
