| **R2** `/mcp` over HTTP | `http.ts`, `httpMain.ts`, Host and Origin guards, limiter, audit to `mcp_audit`, kill and policy and tenant routes (reads and management), the isolation test, matrix cells M1, M2, M3 (or its fallback) and M6 | M1 to M3 and M6 green; **200-interleaved-request isolation green and mutant H1 killed**; one audit row per call; every H mutant killed | 1 |
| **R2b** Compute host | `host/workerHost.ts`, `host/worker.ts`, the bundle and worker hash pin in `mcpRoute.js`, the per-call deadline, per-tenant concurrency, resident quotas, `remote/worker` | the event-loop test (a 250k-voucher GST call in the worker while `/health` and a push answer within 100 ms); a runaway call terminates the worker and the next call reloads; a one-byte change to either bundle file refuses to start; every L mutant of the cache group and H21 killed | 0.8 |
| **R3** Writes in the backend | `write/inProcess.ts`, the write gateway routes, the commit transaction, `assertDocument`, numbering through the real `numbering.js`, the replay-first idempotency over `mcp_commit`, the five switches, the in-transaction authorization re-read and ceilings, the audit insert | the concurrency and numbering suites green on the real-Postgres lane (two confirms give one document, two keys give consecutive numbers, **a failure between reservation and insert leaves the counter unchanged**); exactly one `INSERT INTO change_log` (lint); draft transparency green through the plugin; the remaining W mutants killed | 1.2 |
| **R4** Integration and rehearsal | `scripts/remote-smoke.mjs` (the real stdio binary against the real plugin on a throwaway cluster, through Fastify over TCP), the wiring smoke with the real `db.js` (needs an `npm install` in `backend/`: **the orchestrator decides**), `DEPLOY.md`, the 250,000-voucher EXPLAIN record | end to end on a throwaway cluster: a key, the stdio binary, the plugin, Postgres, a draft saved, the draft promoted in memory and the books balanced; the owner reads `DEPLOY.md` and can follow it | 0.8 |
| **R-iso** Isolated reads (stage 3; the owner's decision, question 12) | `httpMain` hardened as a pass-through service with its own systemd unit (`comhard-mcp`, a dedicated unprivileged user, an env file holding only `COMSKY_BOOKS_URL=http://127.0.0.1:1036`, `MCP_HTTP_HOST` and the proxy settings, **no database URL and no secret**), its `DEPLOY.md` section, and `MCP_PROTOCOL_ROUTE=0` on the backend so only the gateway stays in the sync process | a customer-shaped session through `httpMain` over loopback to the gateway; the unit's environment and files hold no credential; stopping the unit leaves sync untouched; the gateway contract and the protocol matrix pass against it | 1.0 |

**Sync points.** After **C2 and R1:** `RestDataSource` in gateway mode against the real plugin (the contract file is the proof). After **C4 and R3:** preview and confirm through the gateway end to end, which is when the in-memory double of C4 is replaced by the real thing and the W mutants that needed R3 are killed. After both lanes: the orchestrator applies seam S1 and the wiring smoke runs.

**Totals [J, low confidence]:** core lane about **10 engineer-weeks**, remote lane about **5.8** (R0 1.0, R1 1.0, R2 1.0, R2b 0.8, R3 1.2, R4 0.8; plus **1.0** for R-iso, the stage-3 isolation slice), in parallel about **10 weeks** for two engineers. **The smallest release a customer can use** is C0a, C0b, C1 and C3 trimmed to six tools (companies, find, outstandings, ledger statement, financials, refresh): about **5 weeks**, no backend deploy, and it answers the receivables, party, profit and Balance Sheet questions of the launch set (A1 to A4, H1, H4) [R use-cases 2.3]. The remaining 8 read tools add C2 (2 weeks); the backend plugin for the gateway and `/mcp` adds R0 to R2b (3.8); drafts add C4 (2), R3 (1.2) and the rehearsal R4 (0.8), so **writes arrive about 6 engineer-weeks after the plugin work starts** and need the wave-3 backend deployed first.

### 7.5 The seams: the only edits outside `mcp/` and `backend/src/mcpRoute.js`

| Id | File | The change | Needed for | Applied by, and when | Without it |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **S1** | `backend/src/server.js` | one line, directly after `await app.register(restRoutes);` (line 51 today): `await app.register((await import('./mcpRoute.js')).mcpPlugin); // Comsky MCP: dormant unless MCP_ENABLED=1` | the plugin, the gateway, `/mcp`, every write | the orchestrator, after R4's wiring smoke; **inert with the flag off, and with the flag on inert for every tenant that has no `mcp_tenant` row** | stdio still works read-only through `/v1` (`rest-v1`) |
| **A1** | `src/types.ts` (`Voucher.origin?`), `src/ui/admin/AuditLog.tsx` or a Day Book badge, the `master-resave` classification suite, and a server-backed view of change-log rows | show "MCP: <key name>" on a draft and in an audit view. Today the Edit Log reads only the device's own outbox [C AuditLog.tsx:101] and a pulled change writes no outbox row [R audit finding 11], so an MCP draft does not appear in it | the owner's stated aim ("the audit log shows `MCP: <key name>`") | the app team, after wave 3's gate | the stamp still exists in the document (`origin`, the `[AI draft]` narration tag) and in `change_log.device_id`; `GET /mcp/gw/audit` lists it for owners |
| **A2** | a new app screen "AI access" | calls `/mcp/gw/policy/:keyId`, `/tenant`, `/kill`, `/audit` (owner or admin) | owners managing keys without `curl` | the app team, v1.1 | the owner uses `curl` with a bearer token |
| **A3** | `src/ui/admin/ApiKeys.tsx` | the disclosure text of 5.7 at key creation, a "read only" default tick, and the key's MCP policy shown | informed consent | the app team, with A2 | the README carries the text |
| **E1** | `src/state/store.ts` | extract `nextVchNo` and `formatVchNo` (and the Balance Sheet rows) into `src/core/` | removes two of the five mirrors | the wave-3 owner, optional | the mirrors and their drift guards stay |
| **T1** | deployment | TLS on the app and API host | every customer key, the remote endpoint, OAuth | the owner | loopback and tunnel only (D15) |
| **R5** | `backend/src/restPolicy.js redactForRead` | also strip `vaultPin`, `pin` and anything the `SECRET_KEY` pattern matches (`restPolicy.js` is plain JS and cannot import `src/core/splitCompany.ts`: the short pattern is copied, with a test asserting the two stay equal) [R audit finding 9] | the plain `/v1` read path, **which every MCP key can call whatever the MCP design says**: `redactForRead` strips only `vault`, `vaultLegacy`, `security` and `pinHash` [C conflict.js:38-41, restPolicy.js:33-39], so a read key fetching `/v1/companies` (exactly what `rest-v1` does) receives a legacy plaintext `vaultPin`, and `/v1/users` may expose a plaintext `pin` | the backend owner. **A hard prerequisite of stage 3: no customer key exists before it ships** (`--check` fails against a backend that still returns a secret key) | a plaintext PIN or passcode that an old client pushed is readable with any read key |
| **R6** | `backend/src/rest.js:85` | write the delete row with the document's real `cid` [R audit finding 10] | the `rest-v1` ghost | the backend owner | `rest-v1` shows an API-deleted document (6.3 documents it) |
| **K3** | `backend/src/platform.js` (`resolveApiKey`, `v1Auth`) and `backend/src/rest.js` | when an `mcp_key_policy` row exists for the key: refuse an expired or disabled key on `/v1`, and restrict `GET /v1/:collection` and `/v1/:collection/:docId` to the policy's `company_ids` (a `?cid=` outside the list, or a document of another company, is 404). About 25 lines, explicit aliased columns, the same tenant-pairing rule as 1.6 item 3 | making the company allowlist, `expires_at` and `disabled` bind the REST routes that a leaked key also reaches (3.2, 4.7) | the backend owner, **recommended before stage 3** (or the owner accepts in writing that the allowlist is a convenience: question 16) | the stated limitation; revoking the key is the only complete stop |
| **S2** | `backend/src/sync.js` (the push route's `isDeviceId` check, line 182) | refuse a pushed device id that starts with `mcp:` (and `api`), so only the commit transaction can write those stamps | a device-id hint that cannot be forged by an ordinary device, and a rollback query that cannot sweep up a forged row | the backend owner, one line, after wave 3's gate | `mcp_commit` is authoritative anyway; the stamps are hints (D8) |

**The audit's other seams are replaced, not requested.** R1, R2, R3 (paged, head and change reads) become `/mcp/gw/snapshot`, `/head` and `/changes`; R7 (an index) is not needed because the load query keys on the existing `idx_change_doc` [R audit 1.6]; W1 (a validating write) becomes `assertDocument` and the planner inside the commit; W2 (reserve by key) becomes the in-process `reserveNumber`; W4 (attribution) becomes `device_id = 'mcp:<key>'` plus `user_id`; K1 (key expiry, company scope, rate tier, kind) becomes `mcp_key_policy`; K2 (a per-key limiter) becomes the plugin's limiter. **W3** (a base-revision precondition on writes) stays open on purpose: it is needed by any future *alter* tool and by nothing in v1.

**Found in passing, independent of MCP, for the owner** [R audit "Found in passing"]: the webhook URL can be aimed at the VM's own loopback (an egress allow-list is designed and not built); the plan flag `features.api` is read by nothing, so keys work on a plan that says `api: false`; any signed-in member can mint a key although the app's screen is administrator-only; `GET /v1/:collection/:docId` ignores `cid`. None blocks this plan; question 16 asks which to fix first.

### 7.6 Deploying, in stages, with a way back

**The procedure** (the owner supplies the VM's SSH password at the start of the session, as `docs/HANDOFF.md` says; **no secret is written into any file here**). It is the existing recipe plus the lines below:

```
# 0. WRITES ONLY: wave 3 (backend/src/numbering.js + sql/008_voucher_series.sql) must already be committed and deployed.
#    Read-only needs nothing from wave 3.
# 1. build      cd mcp && npm ci && npm run gate && npm run build            # dist/http.mjs, dist/worker.mjs, dist/stdio.mjs; prints the SHA-256 of http.mjs and worker.mjs
# 2. seam S1    (orchestrator) the one line in backend/src/server.js
# 3. ship       rsync backend/ exactly as docs/HANDOFF.md §2 (exclude node_modules and .env);
#               rsync mcp/dist/http.mjs and mcp/dist/worker.mjs -> /home/ubuntu/comhard/mcp/dist/
# 4. env        in backend/.env on the VM (never in git; JWT_SECRET must already be a real secret, the plugin refuses the dev default):
#                 MCP_ENABLED=1
#                 MCP_TOKEN_SECRET=<openssl rand -base64 48>          # required whenever the plugin is on
#                 MCP_BUNDLE_SHA256=<printed by the release script for dist/http.mjs>
#                 MCP_WORKER_SHA256=<printed for dist/worker.mjs>
#                 MCP_ALLOWED_HOSTS=127.0.0.1:1036,localhost:1036      # add the public host:port later
#               later, for writes:
#                 MCP_WRITES_ENABLED=1
#                 MCP_WRITE_KINDS=receipt,payment,contra,journal      # required; sales,purchase only after question 5
#               and per tenant, by owner-pool SQL (never by a route); stage 1 is the owner's own tenant only:
#                 INSERT INTO mcp_tenant(tenant_id, allowed) VALUES ('<tenant uuid>', true) ON CONFLICT (tenant_id) DO UPDATE SET allowed = true;
#                 (for writes also: writes_allowed = true)
# 5. restart    sudo systemctl restart comhard-backend
# 6. verify     curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' http://127.0.0.1:1036/mcp/gw/info   # 401 without a key = the plugin is up
#               curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' http://<the VM's public address>:1036/mcp/gw/info   # from ANOTHER machine: 426 = plain HTTP is refused
#               npx -y @comhard/books-mcp@<ver> --check   (through an SSH tunnel; a read-only key from the app, approved by PUT /mcp/gw/policy/<key id>)
```
The embedded DDL (1.6 item 2) creates `mcp_tenant`, `mcp_key_policy`, `mcp_commit` and `mcp_audit` (and one unique index on `api_key`) at registration, so `npm run migrate` is not part of it (if the owner prefers the repo's convention, the same text moves verbatim to `backend/sql/009_mcp.sql` and `npm run migrate` applies it). Before the first start: `free -m` on the VM, and set `MCP_MAX_VOUCHERS` and `MCP_MAX_RESIDENT` to the values question 7 gives; the compute runs in a worker with a heap limit (D20), so a runaway book kills the worker and not sync, but the main thread, the pages and the commit still share the sync process, which is why the caps are conservative and the flag is the first thing to turn off.

**The stages.** Each has an entry condition, what is done, and a stop condition.

| Stage | Who | What | Stop and roll back when |
|---|---|---|---|
| **0** | the builders | everything on a throwaway cluster; the gate green twice | any gate failure |
| **1** | the owner's own tenant (the hidden `unlimited` plan account with the sample company loaded, per the owner's own account-ops note of 2026-10 [not re-checked here; the seed scripts that made it are `scripts/seed/run.mjs` and `to-sql.mjs`, C]) over an **SSH tunnel** (`ssh -p 1306 -L 1036:127.0.0.1:1036 ubuntu@<vm>`, base URL `http://127.0.0.1:1036`, loopback so D15 allows it) | `allowed` set for this tenant only (SQL); a read-only key, approved by `PUT /mcp/gw/policy/<key id>`; the 20 questions by hand in two clients; read `mcp_audit`; prove that a non-loopback request is refused (7.6 step 6) | a wrong figure (name the engine commit from `basis.engine`), a leak in any result, memory growth on the VM |
| **2** | the same tenant | writes on (wave 3 deployed): `writes_allowed` by SQL, then `PUT /mcp/gw/tenant { writes_enabled: true }`, and `policy.writes` on the key (the key stays read-scope); the parity checklist of 6.7; promote drafts in the app; confirm numbers and the Edit Log behaviour; the accountant's answer on draft invoice numbers (question 5) | a draft that moves a figure, a number gap the accountant rejects, a stale or duplicate number |
| **3** | two or three design-partner customers, read only | **only after all of:** TLS (the server refuses plain HTTP anyway); seam R5 shipped (hard); seam K3 shipped, or the owner's written acceptance of the stated limitation; the isolation decision of question 12 made (default: slice R-iso, reads in a process that holds no secret) and the bundle hashes pinned; counsel's wording (question 13); one approved key per person, its company list chosen explicitly | any customer-visible wrong figure or any doubt about isolation: `POST /mcp/gw/kill` first, then investigate |
| **4** | partners who ask for drafts | writes per tenant, per key | the same |
| **5** | everyone | general availability for the clients that work with a header key; OAuth work starts for claude.ai and ChatGPT if question 17 says so | n/a |

**Rollback, from the quickest.** (1) `POST /mcp/gw/kill` (owner or admin bearer): the tenant's MCP stops within the serving process at once. (2) Unset `MCP_ENABLED` and restart: the plugin registers nothing; the four tables and the one index stay, harmless. (3) Revoke a key in the app. **What a rollback leaves behind:** drafts are ordinary Optional vouchers whose ids are in `mcp_commit` (`SELECT doc_id FROM mcp_commit WHERE tenant_id = ...`; the narration begins `[AI draft]` and `change_log.device_id` begins `mcp:`, but those two are hints that any device could forge, D8); they move no figure; a person deletes any they do not want in the app, and a deleted Sales draft leaves its invoice number unused (2.4.1).

### 7.7 What "done" means, and what stays unproven

**Done** is: the gate green twice with a stable fingerprint; the three manual checks of 6.7 recorded; the snippet check of 5.3 recorded; slice reports whose statuses follow the repo's rule. It is **not** "works with every client".

**Stays unverified until someone does it** (each is a line in the README or `DEPLOY.md`, not silence): any live client; the protocol era each client sends today (the dual-era handler is the hedge, 1.1); the legacy-era route through `handler.fetch` (M3); claude.ai and ChatGPT with an HTTPS port other than 443 and with Anthropic's address range (3.4); the `.mcpb` on Windows and the Node version inside Claude Desktop; the memory the plugin needs on the VM's real tenants; Postgres 16 plans (14 and 17 bracket it); whether a Sales draft's number is acceptable in a GST series; the statutory vocabulary of the TDS output; the DPDP position of sending books to an AI vendor (counsel).

**Top risks and what holds each:** a wrong figure stated confidently (reconciliation to the app, the engine lock, the basis block on every result); a leak through a name or narration (cleaning, labelling, the allowlist-as-schema, the canary test, the red-team harness); a tenant bleed (principal from the verified key only, never from a joined policy row, `AsyncLocalStorage` with a cross-check, tenant-keyed caches, the 200-request isolation test and its mutant); a bad write (drafts only, the planner, `assertDocument`, one `INSERT`, the ceilings of D21, the replay record; the token is an integrity check, not an approval); an outage of sync caused by MCP (the flag, caps, the compute worker and its deadline, the load semaphore, the kill switch; sharing the process with the sync server is the known cost until R-iso); a stolen key (4.7: revoking stops it everywhere, the policy only on MCP, K3 would close the gap).

---

## 8. Questions that genuinely need the owner

Each question is one the builders cannot settle by reading code: money, risk appetite, law, a customer relationship or a machine only the owner has. **Every one has a default and the build proceeds on it**; a different answer changes the named switch or slice, not the architecture. The numbers are referenced from earlier sections (2.4.1 cites question 4, 1.5.3 cites question 7).

**1. TLS: when, and on which hostname?** *Why it is yours:* the VM's ports 80 and 443 belong to another app, so the options are a DNS-01 certificate for a `comhard.in` host served on 1036, or a provider route from 443, and both need DNS or hosting access only you have [C docs/HANDOFF.md:56-62, 89-91]. *Default:* build everything now; **no customer key travels over plain HTTP** (D15). For your own testing use an **SSH tunnel to the VM** (the stdio server accepts a loopback base URL, so the traffic is encrypted by SSH). Customers wait for TLS. *If you decide differently:* a customer on plain HTTP needs **both** `MCP_ALLOW_PLAIN_HTTP=1` on the server (the server refuses non-loopback plain HTTP by itself, whatever the client does: 1.6 item 6) and `COMSKY_MCP_ALLOW_INSECURE_HTTP=1` in the client, a printed warning on every result, and your written acceptance of the risk; I would not.

**2. Drafts or direct posting (D4)?** *Why it is yours:* it is the one control that removes a person from the loop. *Default:* **every AI-created voucher is an Optional draft**, never a Regular one, in v1. It costs the customer one keypress to promote (2.4.1). *If you decide differently:* a per-key `postAs: 'regular'` is a v2 slice needing an opt-in per key, a per-key daily value cap, and a visible undo; it is deliberately not designed further here. A person-in-the-loop approval through MCP elicitation is specified for v1.1 (4.1 item 7) and would make even the draft step explicit.

**3. Which voucher kinds are switched on in the first write release, and what are the ceilings?** *Default:* the code supports `sales`, `purchase`, `receipt`, `payment`, `journal` and `contra`; **`MCP_WRITE_KINDS` has no built-in default and the plugin refuses to start with writes on and the variable unset** (the first pass defaulted it to every kind plus `ledger`, which contradicted this answer). For stage 2 you set **`receipt,payment,contra,journal`**, and `sales,purchase` are added only after the parity checklist (6.7) passes, the rate-source fixtures of 6.6 are green and question 5 is answered. Those four carry no GST arithmetic and no stock, so they are where a first mistake is cheapest; **no entry that needs TDS or TCS is offered, and ledger creation is not in v1.** *The ceilings (D21), which you may lower per key or raise in the environment:* 25 unpromoted AI drafts per company, 10 drafts an hour, 50 a day and ₹1 crore of draft value a day per key, ₹10 crore per voucher. *If you decide differently:* set the variables; nothing else changes.

**4. The `[AI draft]` narration tag.** Every draft's narration begins `[AI draft] ` and stays until a person edits it, so a promoted draft prints that tag on the voucher and in every list that shows narration (2.4.1). *Why it is yours:* it is on your customers' documents. *Default:* **keep the tag**: it is the only visible sign in the app today (seam A1, the badge, is not built), and a person who promotes without looking is exactly who it protects. *Alternatives:* keep the stamp only in the document's `origin` object (invisible until A1 ships), or a shorter `[AI]`. Removing it is a one-line change in `buildVoucher.ts` and the golden.

**5. Draft invoice numbers and gaps (a chartered-accountant question).** A draft Sales or Purchase voucher takes the next number of its series when saved, because the app numbers Optional vouchers the same way [C store.ts:719-735]; a draft a person deletes leaves that number unused (2.4.1). *Why it is yours:* whether a gap in a GST invoice series is acceptable, and how the Documents Issued report treats it, is a professional judgement I could not verify [unverified]. *Default:* drafts take numbers; the confirm result for a Sales draft says so in plain words; **invoice drafts stay off until your accountant answers** (question 3). *If the answer is "not acceptable":* the fallback is to reserve no number at confirm and let the person's promotion assign it, which needs an app change (the promote key does not number today) and is therefore a later slice, not a switch.

**6. Who may create keys, who approves them, plan gating, and is this a paid feature?** Today any signed-in member can mint a key although the app's screen is administrator-only, and the plan flag `features.api` is read by nothing, so a key works on a plan that says `api: false` [R audit findings 8; C platform.js:78-90]. *Default:* **no change to who may mint a key or to plan gating in v1** (that needs a backend edit and changes what existing trial users can do), **but MCP does not accept a key until an owner or administrator approves it** (a policy row with `approved_by`, D22): the key must have the `read` scope only, the approver chooses its companies explicitly, and writes are a separate flag of the approver's. The MCP **management** routes (approval, tenant switch, kill, audit) are owner or admin only. Record the question about plan gating, price nothing. *If you decide differently:* enforcing the plan flag is a three-line change in the plugin's authenticator (and in `rest.js` if you also want `/v1` gated); restricting who may mint keys is a change in `platform.js` and in the app's key screen; both change what existing users can do, so they are yours to schedule.

**7. The VM's memory, and the snapshot ceilings.** The plugin loads a company's books into a worker thread of the backend's own process (about 0.8-1 GB transient per 250,000 vouchers [R audit 1.6, J]; the worker's heap limit turns an overrun into a dead worker, but the process and its systemd unit still have no memory limit) [C backend/deploy/comhard-backend.service]. *What I need from you:* `free -m` on the VM and the voucher count of your largest tenant. *Default until then:* **`MCP_MAX_VOUCHERS=100000`, `MCP_MAX_RESIDENT=3` (at most one per tenant) and `MCP_WORKER_HEAP_MB=1536`**, raised to `300000` only once `free -m` shows at least 4 GB free and the 250,000-voucher EXPLAIN and memory run of 7.4 has been recorded; **`MCP_MAX_RESIDENT=1` and a 768 MB worker heap if less than 2 GB**; start with the flag in stage 1 on your own tenant and watch the process. *Also yours:* whether to add a `MemoryMax=` drop-in to the unit (it would restart the whole backend if exceeded, so I did not assume it).

**8. Do reads keep working after a trial ends (D17)?** *Default:* **yes**, matching `rest.js:25-26` and the "data is never stranded" stance; writes need an active subscription. *If you decide differently:* `MCP_READS_REQUIRE_ACTIVE=1`. Counsel's question 10 in the research is the same question from the legal side.

**9. Do MCP writes fire webhooks (D16)?** *Default:* **no**. A draft is not a business event until a person promotes it, and an edit made in the app fires none today [R audit finding 11]. They do nudge the live stream so devices pull in about a second. *If you decide differently:* add `vouchers.draft_created` as a *new* event name so existing consumers of `vouchers.created` are not surprised.

**10. May a key reveal full GSTIN and PAN?** *Default:* masked (state code and last four) in the output of the `/mcp` tools for every key. `pii: 'full'` exists as a per-key flag that **the tenant's own owner or administrator sets** through the policy route (Comhard holds no such switch: it is the customer's data and the customer's decision); it applies to GSTIN and PAN on two tools and nothing else, and is logged. **Masking is output hygiene, not access control:** the gateway and stdio carry the full identifiers (the engines read them) and REST `/v1` returns them to any read key (3.2). Phone, email, address and bank details are not offered at all in v1. *If you decide differently:* "never" removes the flag from the policy route; "offer it to customers" needs the A2 screen and a consent line; making masking a real boundary would mean running the GST and TDS tools on the server only for masked keys (no stdio for them), which costs the offline and backup-file modes.

**11. Package name, publishing, licence and the `.mcpb`.** *Why it is yours:* a public package name commits you to it, the product name has not cleared a trademark search [C CLAUDE.md, docs/LEGAL-BRIEFING.md], and the licence is a legal text. As of 2026-10-22 `comsky-books-mcp`, `@comsky/books-mcp`, `@comsky/mcp`, `comsky-mcp`, `@comhard/books-mcp` and `comhard-books-mcp` are all unclaimed on npm [npm view]; owning a scope needs an npm organisation, and **the scope should be reserved before any README snippet that names it is shared** (an unclaimed scope can be taken by someone else between the first document and the first publish). *Default:* the package is named **`@comhard/books-mcp`** (the legal entity's name, brand-neutral; `Comsky Books` appears only as a display string from `BRAND`), is **not published** until you create the organisation and approve a proprietary licence file, and design partners get the `npm pack` tarball and its SHA-256 instead; the `.mcpb` is built and tested but distributed only by direct download. *If you decide differently:* the name and `bin` change in two files; `"Tally"` appears in neither.

**12. The plugin inside the backend process, or a second process, and when?** The research suggested a separate service for blast radius [R landscape 7.1]. This design runs the plugin **in** the backend for stages 1 and 2 because a write needs the tenant lock, the in-process number reservation and the same transaction (3.5, 2.4.5), and it puts the compute in a worker thread with a heap limit and a deadline (D20) so a runaway book cannot stall sync. *What a worker does not do:* it is not a security boundary. The bundle runs in the one process that holds `JWT_SECRET`, `DATABASE_URL_OWNER` and the billing secrets [C config.js:17-27]; a poisoned dependency could read them, or `backend/.env` through `fs`. Hash-pinning the bundle (1.1) stops a changed file, not a bad release. *Default:* in-process worker for stages 1 and 2 (your own tenant and your own sample company); **before stage 3 (a customer's key) reads run in a separate process that holds no database credential and no secret** (slice R-iso: `httpMain` over loopback to the gateway, its own systemd unit and unprivileged user, the backend keeping only the gateway and the write routes), and writes stay in the backend. *If you want it differently:* serving customers from the in-process plugin alone is possible (skip R-iso and say so in writing); a second process for writes as well is not recommended, because **live nudges would not reach devices connected to the first process** (the stream hub is per process [C docs/HANDOFF.md trust map]) and the number reservation and the tenant lock live there.

**13. Counsel, and the consent text.** Sending a company's books (and its customers', suppliers' and staff's personal data) to an AI vendor through a customer's chosen client raises DPDP questions the research listed for counsel (data fiduciary and processor roles, notice, cross-border, retention, breach) [R use-cases 4.5]; none of it is legal advice and none is settled. *Default:* the README and the key-creation text of 5.7 and the draft consent wording of the research (Appendix A there) ship as drafts; **no customer other than you is connected until counsel has read them** (stage 3). *Yours:* whom to ask, and whether the tenant must tick a consent box (seam A3).

**14. Audit retention and who can see it.** At the default limit (120 calls a minute) a single misbehaving or leaked key could add up to 172,800 rows a day; read rows are capped at 1 KB of arguments and write rows at 4 KB, so the worst case is on the order of 0.2 GB a day for that one key, and a purge or roll-up job is a v1.1 item you should expect to need. *Default:* `mcp_audit` is never purged in v1 (a one-year minimum is the expectation [Ws, use-cases]), it is inside the nightly `pg_dump`, and the table size is part of the stage-1 check of 7.6; owners and admins read it through `GET /mcp/gw/audit`, which returns masked arguments and never payloads; no customer-facing screen until A2. *Yours:* the retention period, and whether it is part of the data a customer can export.

**15. Which clients to certify at launch, and which customers to start with.** *Default:* certify **Claude Desktop (the `.mcpb` and the config file), Claude Code, Cursor and VS Code** over stdio, and the header-key remote endpoint for Claude Code, Cursor and VS Code once TLS exists; **claude.ai, Claude on a phone and ChatGPT only after OAuth (question 17)**. For stage 3 I need **two or three design-partner customers** (one who works in the desktop app only, for backup-file mode, and one who uses Cursor or VS Code if any does) and your word on which AI products your customers actually use, which I could not find out [R use-cases 8: the demand evidence is one vendor's own user base].

**16. The existing REST gaps: fix them first?** Six items, three that touch MCP directly (R5, K3, S2) and three that exist independently of it (R6, the webhook, any member minting a key): `redactForRead` does not strip `vaultPin` or `pin` (seam R5); `/v1` honours no MCP policy, so the company list, the expiry and the disable switch do not bind it (K3); the REST delete leaves a ghost (R6); a webhook can be aimed at the VM's loopback; any member can mint a key (7.5); and `/sync/push` accepts forged `mcp:` device ids (S2). *Default:* **R5 is a hard prerequisite of stage 3** (three lines; it removes a plaintext PIN or passcode from what any read key can fetch); **K3 is recommended before stage 3** (about 25 lines; without it the company allowlist is described to customers as a convenience, 3.2); R6 and S2 before stage 3 if they are cheap; schedule the other two. They do not block building.

**17. OAuth and the hosted connectors: priority.** *Default:* **after TLS and after stage 3**: Claude on a phone and ChatGPT are the only reach for owners who want to ask their books a question from a phone (there is no Comsky mobile app, 0.2), but it needs a TLS hostname, an authorization server and a consent screen (3.4) and is about 3-5 engineer-weeks [R landscape 7.7, J]. A cheaper first step for Claude only: find out whether your organisation (or a design partner's) has Anthropic's **request-header beta** (`static_headers`, 5.3), which would let claude.ai and the phone app send `X-API-Key` once TLS exists, with no OAuth work (ChatGPT still needs OAuth). First step when you say go on OAuth: a **throwaway connector experiment** (a hello server on the real hostname and port) to learn whether claude.ai and ChatGPT accept a non-443 HTTPS port, which nothing I read answers [unverified]. *Yours:* how important phones are, which decides whether this outranks drafts.

### 8.1 What the build assumes if you answer none of these

TLS later and a tunnel now (and the server refuses plain HTTP itself); drafts only; the four non-GST kinds first, set explicitly (`receipt,payment,contra,journal`); ceilings of 25 open drafts, 10 an hour, 50 a day and ₹1 crore a day; no ledger creation; no TDS or TCS entries; the `[AI draft]` tag kept; invoice drafts off; keys read-scope and approved by an owner or admin, company list chosen explicitly; no plan gating; the conservative memory caps (100,000 vouchers, one resident book per tenant); compute in a worker thread, and reads in a separate secret-free process before the first customer key; reads open after a trial; no webhooks; masked identifiers on `/mcp` output (not a boundary); the package unpublished under `@comhard/books-mcp`; the README text as a draft for counsel; audit kept; the four stdio clients; R5 a hard prerequisite and K3 recommended before customers, with R6 and S2 if cheap; OAuth after stage 3.

---

## Appendix A. The tool index (16 tools)

Hints are `readOnlyHint / destructiveHint / idempotentHint / openWorldHint`; "cost" is the limiter cost of 1.6 item 7 (a cold snapshot load adds one 10). "Slice" is the core-lane slice of 7.4 that builds it.

| # | Name | Class | Hints | Cost | Takes ids or names | Slice |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `books_list_companies` | read | true / false / true / false | 1 | none | C1 |
| 2 | `books_refresh` | read | true / false / true / false | 10 | company id | C1 |
| 3 | `books_find` | read | true / false / true / false | 1 | **a name** (the only tool that takes one) | C1 |
| 4 | `books_outstandings` | read | true / false / true / false | 5 | ids | C1 |
| 5 | `books_ledger_statement` | read | true / false / true / false | 5 | ids | C1 |
| 6 | `books_financials` | read | true / false / true / false | 5 | ids | C1 |
| 7 | `books_cash_position` | read | true / false / true / false | 5 | ids | C2 |
| 8 | `books_sales_purchase_analysis` | read | true / false / true / false | 5 | ids | C2 |
| 9 | `books_stock_report` | read | true / false / true / false | 5 | ids | C2 |
| 10 | `books_gst_summary` | read | true / false / true / false | 5 | ids | C2 |
| 11 | `books_tds_summary` | read | true / false / true / false | 5 | ids | C2 |
| 12 | `books_search_vouchers` | read | true / false / true / false | 5 | ids | C2 |
| 13 | `books_get_voucher` | read | true / false / true / false | 1 | id | C2 |
| 14 | `books_run_checks` | read | true / false / true / false | 5 | ids | C2 |
| 15 | `books_preview_voucher` | write tier, read-only effect | true / false / true / false | 5 | `{ id, name }` references | C4 |
| 16 | `books_confirm_voucher` | write | false / false / true / false | 10 | the same intent plus a token | C4 |



A read-only key lists 1-14. A key that fails any of the first three write switches of 3.2 (process, tenant, key policy) lists 1-14 and nothing else; the kind and the subscription are checked when a write tool is called. `destructiveHint` is false on the confirm tool because no tool in this design alters or removes a stored document; a draft is new. `books_preview_ledger` and `books_confirm_ledger` (rows 17 and 18 of the first pass) are deferred to v1.1 (2.4.6): they are not registered, not in the golden and not in any slice.

**Tags.** `[W]` a page fetched and read; `[Ws]` a search summary only; `[R]` carried from the three research documents, whose authors fetched the page (not re-fetched here); `[C]` read in this repo; `[M]` measured here; `[J]` my judgement; `[unverified]` could not be checked.

**Fetched and read on 2026-10-22 [W]** (SDK documentation on `main`, `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/modelcontextprotocol/typescript-sdk/main/docs/`): `serving/stdio.md`, `testing.md`, `clients/connect.md`, `protocol-versions.md`, `serving/http.md`, `servers/tools.md`, `servers/errors.md`, `serving/legacy-clients.md`, `serving/fastify.md`, `serving/authorization.md`; and the `.mcpb` format's manifest page `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/modelcontextprotocol/mcpb/main/MANIFEST.md`. **`servers/schema-libraries.md` returned 404** on the same day; the `fromJsonSchema` statement in 1.7 therefore rests on search summaries [Ws] (the v2 beta notes and the v2 server API index) and says so. WebFetch returns an extraction, not the raw page, and every page was asked specific questions; where an extraction said a topic was not covered, this document says "not documented" rather than assuming.

**Added by the review round, 2026-10-22 [W]:** `https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/server/tools` (caching hints on `tools/list`; the SHOULD of a serialized-JSON text block next to `structuredContent`; the `InputRequiredResult` and `elicitation/create` flow; tool lists may vary by the authorization presented) and `.../server/utilities/caching` (`ttlMs` and `cacheScope`, which results must carry them, the `private` rule, and the warning that a `public` result may be shared across callers); the SDK page `serving/http.md` again for `hostHeaderValidationResponse`, `originValidationResponse`, `localhostHostValidation`, `localhostOriginValidation` and for `authInfo` (only `clientId` is shown; whether `token` is required is not covered); `https://claude.com/docs/connectors/building/authentication` (`static_headers` beta, `x-api-key` accepted for every connector, one infrastructure for claude.ai, Desktop, mobile, Claude Code and Cowork, Anthropic's range `160.79.104.0/21`); `https://fastify.dev/docs/latest/Reference/Request/` (with `trustProxy`, `request.ip`, `host`, `hostname` and `protocol` come from request headers) and `.../Reply/` (a hijacked reply runs none of the remaining hooks; headers are the caller's job). **Read in this repo or on this machine [C, M]:** the `pg` driver's `lib/result.js` `parseRow` (8.16.0, in an unrelated local copy; the backend pins `^8.13.1`), which assigns `row[field]` in column order, so the last of two same-named columns wins; `core/gstRates.ts` (`resolveItemGst`, `snapshotOf`, `itemFieldsOn`), `VoucherScreen.tsx` (`rowGst`, `tdsSetup`, `detectTcs`), `reports/markOptional.ts` (`applyMark`), `core/gst.ts` (`isIntraState`, `computeItemGst`, `partyGstIssues`), `core/gstr3b.ts` (the `period` issue), `core/tds.ts` (`tdsSummary`) against `core/dashboardData.ts` (`tdsPayable`), `core/dashIndex.ts` (`ahead`, `bankDate`), `core/einvoiceImport.ts` (`portalRecordProblem`), `backend/src/{platform,rest,restPolicy,conflict,server,config,tokens,db,ratelimit,sync}.js`; and `npm view` of the dependencies of `@modelcontextprotocol/node` 2.1.1 and `/server` 2.3.1; **[M]** two read-only esbuild probes (scripts kept in the scratchpad, nothing under the repo) that bundled `core/gst.ts`, `core/gstRates.ts` and `core/gstStates.ts` and ran them: the `itemFieldsOn` recipe against the `resolveItemGst` recipe for an item whose rate comes from its stock group (CGST 0 against CGST 90 plus SGST 90 on Rs 1,000), and `isIntraState` and the state helpers on 'Maharashtra', 'Maharastra', 'MH', '27' and 'Atlantis' (only the first is intra-state; `stateNameOf` resolves '27', `stateCodeOf` resolves names and old spellings, neither resolves 'MH').

**Search summaries [Ws]:** the SDK's v2 testing pages and API index (`https://ts.sdk.modelcontextprotocol.io/v2/testing.html` and the v2 module pages), the v2 beta release notes (standard-schema inputs, `fromJsonSchema`); a third-party note that v1's raw-shape `server.tool()` and v2's `registerTool()` take different schema forms.

## Appendix C. Configuration reference

### C.1 The backend plugin (`backend/.env` on the VM; read after `./config.js` has loaded the file)

| Variable | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `MCP_ENABLED` | unset | exactly `1` enables the plugin; anything else registers nothing |
| `MCP_WRITES_ENABLED` | unset | exactly `1` enables the write tier; also requires `MCP_WRITE_KINDS`, an importable `numbering.js` and the `voucher_series` table, or registration throws |
| `MCP_TOKEN_SECRET` | none | at least 32 bytes, base64 or hex; **never defaulted and required whenever `MCP_ENABLED=1`**; HKDF derives the confirmation-token, cursor and audit-address subkeys from it; changing it invalidates outstanding tokens (10 minutes) and cursors (15 minutes) |
| `MCP_BUNDLE` | `../../mcp/dist/http.mjs` from `backend/src` | path of the bundle the plugin imports (`worker.mjs` is read from the same directory) |
| `MCP_BUNDLE_SHA256`, `MCP_WORKER_SHA256` | none | **required**: the SHA-256 of `dist/http.mjs` and `dist/worker.mjs`, printed by the release script; a mismatch fails registration (1.1) |
| `MCP_REQUIRE_SECURE` | `1` | the secure-transport rule of 1.6 item 6 (loopback peer, or a declared TLS proxy with `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`); anything else is `426` before the key is read |
| `MCP_TRUSTED_PROXIES` | empty | comma list of TCP peer addresses of a TLS-terminating proxy whose `X-Forwarded-Proto` and (right-most) `X-Forwarded-For` are believed |
| `MCP_ALLOW_PLAIN_HTTP` | unset | `1` lifts the secure-transport rule; echoed in `/mcp/gw/info`; needs the owner's written acceptance (question 1) |
| `MCP_PROTOCOL_ROUTE` | `1` | `0` leaves only `/mcp/gw/*` in the backend (stage 3 with slice R-iso serves `/mcp` from the separate process) |
| `MCP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` | empty | comma list; a request with an `Origin` header outside it is 403 (non-browser clients send none) |
| `MCP_ALLOWED_HOSTS` | empty | comma list of acceptable **raw** `Host` header values; **required unless `HOST` is a loopback address** (registration throws otherwise); the stage-1 value is `127.0.0.1:1036,localhost:1036` |
| `MCP_READS_REQUIRE_ACTIVE` | unset | `1`: reads need an active subscription too (question 8) |
| `MCP_MAX_VOUCHER_AMOUNT` | `100000000` | rupees; a key's own limit can only lower it |
| `MCP_MAX_VOUCHERS` | `100000` | snapshot ceiling per company; question 7 raises it once the VM is measured |
| `MCP_MAX_RESIDENT` | `3` | snapshots kept in memory (at most one per tenant) |
| `MCP_MAX_RESIDENT_MB` | `1024` | resident-memory budget for snapshots (running sum of loaded JSON bytes) |
| `MCP_MAX_BYTES_PER_TABLE`, `MCP_MAX_DOC_BYTES` | `134217728`, `262144` | per-table download cap (every source) and per-document cap (a larger document is quarantined) |
| `MCP_WORKERS`, `MCP_WORKER_HEAP_MB` | `1`, `1536` | the number of compute workers and each one's heap limit (`resourceLimits`) |
| `MCP_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS` | `30000` | per-call deadline; on expiry the worker is terminated and restarted |
| `MCP_MAX_OPEN_DRAFTS` | `25` | open (unpromoted) AI drafts per company (`OPEN_DRAFT_LIMIT`) |
| `MCP_MAX_VALUE_PER_DAY` | `10000000` | rupees of draft value a day per key (`VALUE_LIMIT`); a key's policy can only lower it |
| `MCP_CACHE_TTL_SEC` | `60` | freshness window before a `head` check |
| `MCP_WRITE_KINDS` | **none: required when writes are enabled** | comma list drawn from `sales,purchase,receipt,payment,journal,contra` (`ledger` is not accepted in v1: registration throws); other kinds answer `KIND_DISABLED`; the stage-2 value is `receipt,payment,contra,journal` (question 3) |
| `MCP_RATE_PER_MIN`, `MCP_RATE_BURST` | `120`, `40` | per-key token bucket |
| `MCP_WRITES_PER_HOUR`, `MCP_WRITES_PER_DAY` | `10`, `50` | per-key draft budgets, counted from `mcp_commit` (a key's policy may lower them) |
| `MCP_AUDIT_ARGS_BYTES` | `4096` | cap on the stored arguments of a **write** call; a read call is capped at a quarter of it (1,024) |

### C.2 The stdio entry

| Variable or flag | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `COMSKY_BOOKS_URL` | none (required unless a backup file is given) | base address of the books; no default is baked in, so no plain-HTTP default ships |
| `COMSKY_API_KEY` | none | the key; **never a command-line argument** |
| `COMSKY_COMPANY_ID` / `--company` | none | default company when the key sees several |
| `COMSKY_BACKUP_FILE` / `--backup` | none | offline mode (5.4) |
| `COMSKY_MCP_ALLOW_INSECURE_HTTP` | unset | `1` allows a non-loopback `http://` URL, with a warning on every result |
| `COMSKY_MCP_READ_ONLY` / `--read-only` | unset | hide the write tools even when the key allows them |
| `COMSKY_MCP_LOG` | `error` | `error`, `info` or `debug`; stderr only |
| `COMSKY_MCP_CACHE_TTL_SEC` | `60` | freshness window |
| `COMSKY_MCP_MAX_VOUCHERS` | `150000` | ceiling on a laptop |
| `--check`, `--version`, `--help` | | setup check; build version; usage |

Corporate proxies: Node's built-in `fetch` does not use an HTTP proxy unless the Node version and environment enable it, which depends on the Node release [unverified]; a customer behind a proxy may need the gateway reached from a network without one. The README says so.

### C.3 `httpMain` (tests and the optional standalone service)

`httpMain` is a **pass-through**: it holds no key and no database credential and forwards each request's own `X-API-Key` to the gateway at `COMSKY_BOOKS_URL` (the backend's loopback address, or an `https://` URL), so a caller can read only what its own key can. It binds `MCP_HTTP_HOST` (default `127.0.0.1`; `MCP_HTTP_PORT`, default random in tests) and **refuses to start on a non-loopback bind unless `MCP_ALLOWED_HOSTS` is set**. It applies the same secure-transport, `Origin` and raw-`Host` guards as the plugin (`MCP_REQUIRE_SECURE`, `MCP_TRUSTED_PROXIES`, `MCP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, `MCP_ALLOWED_HOSTS`, with the SDK's own helpers), a counting body reader (256 KiB) and a request deadline (`MCP_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`).

## Appendix D. Error codes

Every code the code base may raise is here; a lint fails when `ToolError('CODE'` names one that is not (6.2). A tool failure is `isError: true` with the text `CODE: message. hint`; on the gateway the same code is the body's `error.code` with the HTTP status shown. **Messages are fixed strings and ids; they never echo an argument value, an exception message or a database error, and they echo a document field only in the four codes marked † below, where the name is cleaned, cut to 80 characters, framed « » and followed by the notice sentence (2.1).**

| Code | HTTP | Retry | Raised when | Hint the text gives |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `INVALID_ARGUMENT` | 400 | no | a schema failure | which field and which constraint, never the value |
| `INVALID_PERIOD` | 400 | no | one of `from`/`to` alone; `from` after `to`; a window before `booksFrom` | give both dates or a preset |
| `RANGE_TOO_LARGE` | 400 | no | a window over 36 months | narrow it |
| `STALE_CURSOR` | 400 | repeat the first call | a cursor that expired, was tampered with, or belongs to another tool, arguments, tenant or key (a refresh in between does not invalidate it: cursors are keyset, 1.5.3) | repeat the first call |
| `AMBIGUOUS_COMPANY` † | 400 | no | the key sees several companies and none was named | the ids and names (framed) |
| `NOT_FOUND` | 404 | no | no such record in this company | call `books_find` |
| `OUT_OF_SCOPE` | 403 | no | a company this key may not see (text identical to a missing one) | call `books_list_companies` |
| `AUTH_FAILED` | 401 | no | a malformed, unknown, revoked, disabled or expired key, or a tenant for which AI access is not enabled | one sentence, not saying which |
| `KEY_TOO_BROAD` | 403 | no | the key has the REST `write` scope (D22) | make a new key with Read only ticked |
| `KEY_NOT_APPROVED` | 403 | no | the key has no approved policy row | ask an owner or administrator to approve it for AI access |
| `INSECURE_TRANSPORT` | 426 | no | a plain-HTTP request from a non-loopback peer (D15) | use `https://` or an SSH tunnel |
| `WRITES_DISABLED` | 403 | no | a write tool called while the process, tenant or key switch of 3.2 is off | writes are not enabled for this connection |
| `KIND_DISABLED` | 403 | no | a voucher kind not in `MCP_WRITE_KINDS`, or `ledger` (not a v1 kind) | which kinds are enabled |
| `SUBSCRIPTION_READ_ONLY` | 402 | no | a write on a subscription that cannot sync | renew to create drafts |
| `RATE_LIMITED` | 429 | yes, after `retry_after_seconds` | a bucket is empty | wait, and ask for less |
| `BOOK_TOO_LARGE` | 422 | no | the company exceeds `maxVouchers` | the count and the limit |
| `DATA_UNAVAILABLE` | 503 | yes | the source could not be read in time | try again shortly |
| `UPDATE_REQUIRED` | 426 | no | the stdio client is older than `min_client`, or newer than the gateway understands | the one command to run |
| `UNSUPPORTED_COMBINATION` | 400 | no | e.g. `purchases` grouped by `item` | the supported combinations |
| `UNSUPPORTED_FEATURE` | 422 | no | a write needing batches, export, SEZ, reverse charge, cost centres, discounts, **TDS or TCS**, or a Manual-numbered type | what to do in the app |
| `FIELD_NOT_FOR_KIND` | 400 | no | `"lines" is not used by kind "receipt"` | the allowed fields |
| `FIELD_REQUIRED` | 400 | no | a required field of the kind is missing | the field |
| `NAME_MISMATCH` † | 400 | no | a `Ref`'s name differs from the book's | the book's real name (framed) |
| `WRONG_LEDGER_CLASS` | 400 | no | a party or bank ledger of the wrong class for the kind | the ledger's class |
| `AMBIGUOUS_TAX_LEDGER` † | 400 | no | several tax ledgers of one type and none chosen | their ids and names (framed) |
| `NEEDS_MASTER` | 400 | no | tax is due and there is no tax ledger | create it in the app |
| `PERIOD_LOCKED` | 400 | no | the date is on or before the period lock | choose another date |
| `DATE_OUT_OF_RANGE` | 400 | no | before `booksFrom`, or more than 31 days ahead | the limits |
| `INVALID_PLACE_OF_SUPPLY` | 400 | no | `place_of_supply` is not an Indian state name or code | the candidate states |
| `WRONG_VOUCHER_TYPE` | 400 | no | `voucher_type_id` is not a type of the kind's base | the ids of the types of that base |
| `OPEN_DRAFT_LIMIT` | 409 | no | the company already holds the maximum number of unpromoted AI drafts | promote or delete the AI drafts in the app first |
| `VALUE_LIMIT` | 429 | after the day rolls over | this key's draft value for the day would exceed its cap | the cap and what is used |
| `AMOUNT_NOT_POSITIVE` | 400 | no | an entry or line of zero or less | |
| `AMOUNT_TOO_LARGE` | 400 | no | over the cap (the key's or the global) | the cap |
| `BILL_REF_NOT_FOUND` † | 400 | no | an `against_bills` reference that is not open | the open references (framed) |
| `BILL_OVER_ALLOCATED` | 400 | no | allocations exceed the voucher amount | |
| `DUPLICATE_SUPPLIER_INVOICE` | 400 | no | the same party and supplier invoice number are already booked | the existing voucher id |
| `DUPLICATE_LEDGER_NAME` (v1.1) | 400 | no | a ledger name or alias already used, or a near-duplicate of one (2.4.6) | the existing id |
| `INVALID_GSTIN`, `INVALID_PAN` (v1.1) | 400 | no | failed the check character or format | |
| `NUMBERING_MANUAL` | 422 | no | the voucher type is Manual-numbered | number it in the app |
| `NUMBER_SERIES_FULL` | 422 | no | the series is at its limit | |
| `TOKEN_INVALID` | 400 | no | missing, malformed, wrong signature, wrong key, company, kind, arguments or idempotency key | preview again |
| `TOKEN_EXPIRED` | 400 | no | older than 10 minutes **and the intent was not already committed** (an intent that was committed replays whatever the token's age) | preview again |
| `PREVIEW_STALE` | 409 | no | the books changed so the entry would no longer be the one previewed | preview again |
| `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` | 409 | no | the key was used for a different entry | use a new key |
| `ALREADY_REMOVED` | 409 | no | the entry for this key was saved and later deleted | use a new key; a removed draft is never resurrected |
| `INTERNAL_ERROR` | 500 | maybe | anything else, or an output that failed its allowlist schema | `(ref xxxxxxxx)`; the cause is in the server log under that reference |

## Appendix E. The gateway wire shapes (`gateway: 1`)

**Common.** Base `/mcp/gw`. Data routes authenticate with `X-API-Key` only (never a query parameter) and need an approved, read-scope key; management routes with `Authorization: Bearer <dashboard JWT>` and the `owner` or `admin` role. **Every route first passes the secure-transport, `Origin` and raw-`Host` guards (1.6 item 6): a plain-HTTP request from a non-loopback peer is `426 INSECURE_TRANSPORT` before the key is read.** JSON in and out, `Cache-Control: no-store`. The stdio client also sends `X-Comsky-Client: comsky-books-mcp/<version>`, which is cleaned, capped at 64 characters, logged and **not trusted**. Errors are `{ "error": { "code": "...", "message": "...", "retry_after_seconds": 12 } }` with the status of Appendix D (and a `Retry-After` header on 429). The contract suite `test/contract/gateway.contract.ts` is the executable form of this appendix.

| Route | Request | Response |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /mcp/gw/info` | nothing | `{ "gateway": 1, "min_client": "1.0.0", "server": { "name": "...", "version": "...", "engine_commit": "..." }, "principal": { "key": "3f9a1c2b", "name": "Claude Desktop, Priya", "scopes": ["read"], "via": "api-key", "can_write": false, "pii": "masked", "expires_at": null, "companies": 2 }, "tenant": { "mcp_enabled": true, "writes_enabled": false }, "limits": { "page_docs": 1000, "max_vouchers": 300000 } }`. `key` is the first 8 characters of the key id, never the secret; `server.plain_http_allowed` is true only when `MCP_ALLOW_PLAIN_HTTP=1`. `can_write` is the AND of the process, tenant and key switches of 3.2 (the kind and the subscription are checked per call). 401 for a bad key, 403 `KEY_TOO_BROAD` or `KEY_NOT_APPROVED`, 426 over plain HTTP; the answer always carries `content-type: application/json` and `gateway: 1` (the client treats nothing else as the gateway); 404 on a backend without the plugin (the stdio client then uses `rest-v1`) |
| `GET /mcp/gw/companies` | nothing | `{ "companies": CompanyRow[] }` (1.5.1), limited to the key's allowlist, with `lockedThrough` as the period lock (a date, `null`, or the string `"unknown"`) |
| `GET /mcp/gw/head` | nothing | `{ "seq": 184233 }`, the tenant's `MAX(seq)` (tenant-wide, so a company-restricted key can see it move: 1.5.4) |
| `GET /mcp/gw/snapshot?cid=&table=&after=&upto=&limit=` | `table` is one of the 13 `LOAD_TABLES` or `company`; `limit` at most 1,000; the first page omits `after` and `upto` | `{ "cid": "...", "table": "ledgers", "upto": 184233, "docs": [ { "id": "...", "cid": "...", ... } ], "next": "<last doc id>" \| null }`. The server fixes `upto` on the first page and the caller sends it back on every later page, so all pages of all tables are one consistent read. Documents are **projected** (the allowlist of `fields.ts`; only documents whose latest revision is a put) and **redacted** (`redactForRead` plus `SECRET_KEY`) before they leave, and the pre-count refuses a book above the caps with `BOOK_TOO_LARGE` before the first page. A page is cut at 8 MiB of JSON (fewer docs, `next` still set). A company outside the allowlist is `OUT_OF_SCOPE`; `after` and `upto` are validated |
| `GET /mcp/gw/changes?cid=&since=&upto=&limit=` | `since` is the caller's last `seq` | `{ "upto": 184290, "rows": [ { "seq": 184240, "table": "vouchers", "op": "put", "id": "...", "doc": { ... } } ], "more": false }`; `op: "delete"` rows carry no `doc`; rows of tables outside `LOAD_TABLES` are skipped but `upto` still advances; a `cid = ''` delete row is returned only for a document this company once held (1.5.4); **or** `{ "too_many": true, "upto": 184290 }` when more than 20,000 rows lie after `since` (the caller then does a full load) |
| `POST /mcp/gw/write/preview` | `{ "kind": "voucher", "intent": { ... } }` (body at most 64 KiB; `"ledger"` is reserved for v1.1 and answers `KIND_DISABLED`) | `PreviewResult` (2.4.5), status 200 **even when `ok` is false** (the `blocking` list explains); 403 `WRITES_DISABLED` when the process, tenant or key switch is off |
| `POST /mcp/gw/write/commit` | `{ "kind": "voucher", "intent": { ... }, "token": "..." }` | `CommitResult` (2.4.5); errors `TOKEN_*`, `PREVIEW_STALE`, `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`, `ALREADY_REMOVED`, `SUBSCRIPTION_READ_ONLY`, `PERIOD_LOCKED`, `OPEN_DRAFT_LIMIT`, `VALUE_LIMIT`, `RATE_LIMITED` as in Appendix D; a replay (`replayed: true`) is answered before any validation |
| `GET /mcp/gw/policy/:keyId` (bearer) | nothing | `{ "key_id": "...", "name": "...", "scopes": ["read"], "company_ids": ["..."] \| null, "writes": false, "pii": "masked", "expires_at": null, "disabled": false, "limits": { "readsPerMin": 120, "writesPerHour": 10, "writesPerDay": 50, "maxVoucherAmount": null } }`; a key of another tenant is `NOT_FOUND`, and so is a key with no policy row yet (the `PUT` creates it); the body also carries `approved_by` and `approved_at` |
| `PUT /mcp/gw/policy/:keyId` (bearer) | any of `company_ids`, `writes`, `pii`, `expires_at`, `disabled`, `limits` (each validated; company ids must belong to the tenant; `limits` can only lower the process defaults). **The first `PUT` for a key is its approval and must state `company_ids`** (an array, or the string `"all"`); it sets `approved_by` to the caller. The key is looked up through `api_key.id = :keyId AND api_key.tenant_id = <the caller's tenant>`, so another tenant's key is `NOT_FOUND` and is never written | the policy as above; takes effect within 30 s, at once on the serving process |
| `GET` and `PUT /mcp/gw/tenant` (bearer) | `PUT` body `{ "enabled"?: boolean, "writes_enabled"?: boolean }` (`writes_enabled: true` is refused unless Comhard set `writes_allowed`; `allowed` and `writes_allowed` cannot be written here) | `{ "allowed": true, "writes_allowed": false, "enabled": true, "writes_enabled": false, "epoch": 3 }`; a tenant with no row is `NOT_FOUND` |
| `POST /mcp/gw/kill` (bearer) | nothing | `{ "ok": true, "epoch": 4 }`: sets `enabled` false, bumps the epoch, clears this tenant's snapshots and auth caches in this process |
| `GET /mcp/gw/audit?limit=50&before=<id>` (bearer) | `limit` at most 200 | `{ "rows": [ { "id": 981, "at": "2026-10-22T10:14:07+05:30", "key_name": "...", "tool": "books_outstandings", "cid": "...", "outcome": "ok", "ms": 214, "doc_ids": [], "args": { ... } } ], "next": 980 \| null }`; arguments are the masked 4 KB record, never results |

**The protocol endpoint.** `POST /mcp` (Streamable HTTP, stateless, dual-era); `GET` and `DELETE` answer 405; `X-API-Key` required (an approved, read-scope key); body at most 256 KiB; a plain-HTTP request from a non-loopback peer is `426` before the key is read; a request with an `Origin` header outside `MCP_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, or a raw `Host` outside `MCP_ALLOWED_HOSTS`, is 403; a request that ran out of tokens is `RATE_LIMITED` as a tool error for `tools/call` and 429 otherwise; `tools/list` and `server/discover` answer `cacheScope: "private"`. Unknown paths under `/mcp/` are 404 JSON.

## Appendix F. Review log (third pass, 2026-10-22)

Two review lenses read the second-pass spec adversarially: a **security** lens (24 findings: S1 to S24, in the order they were reported) and a **correctness and protocol** lens (20 findings: C1 to C20). Each finding was re-checked before it was applied: against the repo (`backend/src`, `src/core`, `src/ui`, `src/types.ts`), against the protocol and SDK pages named in Appendix B, and, where a claim was about behaviour, by a read-only probe (the installed `pg` driver's source for S1; a bundled run of the real GST engines for C1 and C7). **No finding failed to reproduce in whole.** Several were corrected, narrowed or only partly adopted; those are listed after the table, with the reason. Statuses: **applied** (the finding stood and the fix is in the text), **applied, corrected** (the finding stood, its proposed fix did not and was changed), **partly adopted** (a residual is stated in the text), **duplicate** (the same defect as another row).

| Id | Sev | Finding in one line | Re-check | Disposition and where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | blocker | The key-and-policy `LEFT JOIN ... p.*` lets a same-named `tenant_id` column win: a key with no policy resolves to tenant null; a policy `PUT` could store another tenant | Reproduced from the spec's own DDL (both tables have `tenant_id` and `updated_at`) and from `pg` 8.16.0 `parseRow` (last column wins) | **Applied.** Three explicit aliased queries, tenant only from `api_key.tenant_id`, composite foreign key, `PUT` through `api_key.id AND tenant_id = caller` (1.6 items 2, 3, 5; Appendix E; tests 6.2 and 6.9; mutants H15, H17) |
| S2 | blocker | `/v1` reads no policy, and MCP writes needed the REST `write` scope, so a drafting key is a full REST write key and company, expiry and disable do not bind `/v1` | Reproduced [C rest.js:31-91, platform.js:21-30] | **Applied, different mechanism.** D22 (MCP accepts only read-scope keys, `KEY_TOO_BROAD`), policy-binding statement (3.2), stolen-key table (4.7), seam K3 for `/v1`. Not adopted: "mint MCP keys with a scope `/v1` refuses" (the key screen and `POST /api/keys` accept only `read` or `write` [C platform.js:83]; it would edit `platform.js` and the app) |
| S3 | major | Preview then confirm is an integrity check, not a human approval; the token is not one-time and the nonce is unchecked | Reproduced (the same model calls both tools; no step needs a person) | **Applied.** D4, D5, D21, 2.4.4 step 11, 2.4.5 token (nonce removed, "replay-safe"), 4.1 item 7, red-team and acceptance wording (6.5, 6.7). **Partly adopted:** the elicitation step is specified for v1.1, not built (client support and the SDK API are unverified) |
| S4 | major | `MCP_WRITE_KINDS` defaults to every kind plus `ledger`, contradicting Q3 and 8.1; a lookalike ledger passes the exact-match check | Reproduced (Appendix C against Q3) | **Applied.** No built-in default; startup refuses writes without the variable; stage-2 value `receipt,payment,contra,journal` (D21, 1.6 item 1, Appendix C, Q3, 7.6); ledger creation removed from v1 (C6) |
| S5 | major | `mcp_tenant.enabled` defaults true and an absent row is unspecified: the process flag turns MCP on for every tenant | Reproduced | **Applied.** `allowed` and `writes_allowed` (Comhard only, default false), absent row = disabled (D14, 1.6 items 2 and 3, 7.6 stage 1, test `remote/tenant-gate`) |
| S6 | major | Plain HTTP is refused only by the stdio client; the server takes `X-API-Key` over HTTP on every route | Reproduced [C server.js:29, HANDOFF] | **Applied.** `MCP_REQUIRE_SECURE` (1.6 item 6): loopback peer or a declared TLS proxy, never `request.ip` or `request.protocol`; `426 INSECURE_TRANSPORT`; verified in stage 1; mutant H13 |
| S7 | major | `pii: 'masked'` cannot bind the gateway or stdio, which carry full GSTIN and PAN | Reproduced | **Applied, corrected.** Stated plainly (3.2, 4.3, Q10, 5.7); canary asserts masking on `/mcp` output and the projection on the gateway. **Rejected:** replacing GSTIN and PAN in gateway documents, because the GST and TDS engines read the full values and stdio runs on the holder's machine |
| S8 | major | Minimisation is a blocklist: unknown keys kept; `einvoices` unclassified; Company contact fields and entry instrument numbers kept | Reproduced in all four parts [C types.ts:107-262, 880-943, 1093-1121] | **Applied, corrected.** Projection allowlists with a classification test over every loaded interface, a trace test and a never list (D11, 1.5.2 step 2). Correction: the proposed e-invoice projection would make `burnedNumber` skip every portal row (it calls `portalRecordProblem`, which reads the IRN, Ack and GSTIN [C einvoiceImport.ts:343]), so the projection carries a computed `credible` flag and the numbering floor uses a mirror (1.4) |
| S9 | major | `cleanText` leaves « and »; error texts and engine issue texts echo tenant strings | Reproduced [C gst.ts:527-541; Appendix D hints] | **Applied.** Delimiters replaced (4.2); four error codes frame a cleaned name; engine sentences never forwarded (2.1, `format/issues.ts`); `notice` in structured content; corpus and assertions (6.5); mutants F11 to F13 |
| S10 | major | Loads and engines run on the sync server's single event loop; no per-tenant fairness, byte quota or pre-count | Reproduced | **Applied.** D20 (worker thread, heap limit, deadline, one call per tenant), caps for every table, resident memory budget, pre-count, 100,000-voucher default (1.5.3, 1.5.4, 1.6 item 7, 6.2, slice R2b) |
| S11 | major | No pre-auth throttle; unbounded negative cache on a two-connection owner pool | Reproduced [C db.js:10, ratelimit.js:42-45] | **Applied.** Key-shape check before any lookup, bounded LRU, one owner lookup at a time, per-peer and global pre-auth buckets (1.6 item 3; test 6.2; mutant H20) |
| S12 | major | The bundle runs in the process that holds `JWT_SECRET`, the owner database URL and billing secrets | Reproduced [C config.js:17-27] | **Partly adopted.** Hash pin, input-list diff, stated limits of a worker (D20, 1.1, 5.1, Q12); a separate secret-free process is the default **before stage 3** (slice R-iso), not for stages 1 and 2 |
| S13 | major | `rest-v1` makes the live sync process scan and serialise a whole table per reload | Reproduced [C rest.js:35-44] | **Applied.** TTL 15 minutes, refresh limited, 32 MiB cap, owner-test and small books only (1.5.4, 7.1). No new `/v1` route (off limits) |
| S14 | major | `redactForRead` leaves a plaintext `vaultPin` and `pin` readable with a read key; R5 is only "recommended" | Reproduced [C conflict.js:38-41] | **Applied.** R5 is a hard prerequisite of stage 3; `--check` fails against a backend that returns a secret (7.5, 1.5.4, 5.2, Q16) |

Stated: approval does not restrict what the same holder reads through `/v1` |

| S18 | minor | Replay and idempotency gaps: the first edit drops `origin`; "valid exactly once" against replay; cross-key id collisions; a revoked key can commit for 30 s; checks outside the lock | Reproduced (a to e) | **Applied.** `mcp_commit` replay record, key id in the document id, "replay-safe", authorization re-read and re-checks inside the lock (D6, 2.4.5, 6.6) |
| S19 | minor | `voucher_type_id` base and the sales ledger's group are never checked | Reproduced | **Applied.** 2.4.4 steps 4 and 5; `WRONG_VOUCHER_TYPE`, `WRONG_LEDGER_CLASS` |
| S20 | minor | Audit weaknesses: PII in args, unsalted address hash, uncapped client string, mutable rows, in-memory budgets, no failed-auth record | Reproduced | **Applied.** No free text in args, salted HMAC, cleaned 64-character client string, `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE`, budgets from `mcp_commit`; failed auth counted in the process log (1.6 items 2, 3, 7; 4.5) |
| S21 | minor | The `mcp:` device id and `origin` can be forged by any device or REST write | Reproduced [C conflict.js:94, sync.js:182] | **Applied.** Stamps are hints; `mcp_commit` is authoritative; outputs say "claimed"; seam S2 reserves the prefix (D8, 2.4.5, 3.3, 7.5) |

| S23 | minor | `JWT_SECRET` defaults to a dev value; npm scope unreserved; Q10 misattributes who sets `pii: 'full'` | Reproduced [C config.js:27] | **Applied.** Registration refuses the default secret (1.6 item 1); scope reserved first (Q11); Q10 rewritten |
| S24 | minor | Hostile ids and keys as document data; a bare HTTP 200 treated as the gateway | Reproduced [C server.js:56-66] | **Applied.** Hostile-document quarantine and `Map` indexes (1.5.2 step 3), JSON and `gateway: 1` mode detection (1.5.4), tests (6.5) |

Sales and purchase also stay off by default (Q3) |

| C3 | major | The replay lookup comes after validation, so a retry of a purchase is blocked by its own draft | Reproduced (the order in 2.4.4 and 2.4.5) | **Applied.** Replay first (D6, 2.4.5 flow step 0); after-expiry replay decided: it replays |
| C4 | major | The text carries 15 rows while a page has up to 100, and the cursor skips the rest | Reproduced; the SHOULD on a serialized-JSON text block verified [W tools page] | **Applied.** The page is cut to the text budget so text and structured rows are the same rows; the deviation from the SHOULD is stated (2.1, 2.1.2) |

### F.1 Parts of findings that were not adopted, or were adopted differently

* **S2, "mint MCP keys with a scope `/v1` refuses".** Not adopted: the existing key screen and `POST /api/keys` allow only `read` and `write`, so it needs an edit to `platform.js` and the app, which this design does not make. The same effect for writes comes from D22; for reads it is seam K3.
* **S7, "replace GSTIN and PAN in gateway documents".** Rejected: the GST and TDS engines read the full values.
* **S8, the e-invoice projection.** Adopted with a correction (`credible`), because the proposed minimal row would silently disable the numbering floor.
* **S12, "run the library in a separate process or worker with only an app-role connection".** Adopted in two steps: worker thread plus hash pin for stages 1 and 2; separate secret-free process (slice R-iso) before stage 3. A worker thread is stated not to be a security boundary.
* **S13 and C9, "add a head route".** Not adopted: a new `/v1` route edits `rest.js`; the gateway is the answer, and `rest-v1` is limited to small books.
* **S22.** Partly: the tenant-wide counter cannot be per company without breaking consistent reads; tombstone ids are fixed.
* **S3.** The elicitation step is a v1.1 specification, not code; v1 rests on drafts and ceilings.
* **C2.** Narrowed to the exact conditions of the screen's assist, not to every ledger that has any TDS field.
* **C7.** The proposed normalisation of abbreviations was dropped after the probe: the helpers do not resolve them.
* **C10.** A defensive rule instead of a fact; the second manifest is a fallback.
* **C18.** Keyset cursors instead of keeping an old snapshot.

### F.2 What changed in the numbers the builders will meet

Tools: **16** (14 read, 2 write; was 18). Decisions added: D20 (compute isolation and bundle pin), D21 (ceilings), D22 (read-scope approved keys); D4, D5, D6, D8, D11, D13, D14, D15 rewritten. Tables: `mcp_tenant` (4 booleans), `mcp_key_policy` (approval, composite foreign key), **`mcp_commit` (new)**, `mcp_audit` (append-only), one unique index on `api_key`. New env: `MCP_BUNDLE_SHA256`, `MCP_WORKER_SHA256`, `MCP_REQUIRE_SECURE`, `MCP_TRUSTED_PROXIES`, `MCP_ALLOW_PLAIN_HTTP`, `MCP_PROTOCOL_ROUTE`, `MCP_MAX_RESIDENT_MB`, `MCP_MAX_BYTES_PER_TABLE`, `MCP_MAX_DOC_BYTES`, `MCP_WORKER_HEAP_MB`, `MCP_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`, `MCP_MAX_OPEN_DRAFTS`, `MCP_MAX_VALUE_PER_DAY`; `MCP_TOKEN_SECRET` and `MCP_WRITE_KINDS` now required, `MCP_MAX_VOUCHERS` default 100,000. New error codes: `KEY_TOO_BROAD`, `KEY_NOT_APPROVED`, `INSECURE_TRANSPORT`, `INVALID_PLACE_OF_SUPPLY`, `WRONG_VOUCHER_TYPE`, `OPEN_DRAFT_LIMIT`, `VALUE_LIMIT`. Seams: S2 and K3 added, R5 made a hard prerequisite. Slices: R2b (compute host, 0.8 weeks) and R-iso (isolated reads, 1.0, the owner's decision) added; R0 grows to 1.0; remote lane about 5.8 weeks plus R-iso. Mutants: L12 redefined, L13 to L18, F11 to F16, T19 to T22, W19 to W28, H12 redefined and H13 to H23 added.

### F.3 Still not verified after this round

No live MCP client of any kind; whether any client honours elicitation or a `static_headers` credential in practice; the SDK's own defaults for cache hints and for `structuredContent` without an `outputSchema`; whether `authInfo.token` is required; how Claude Desktop passes an unset optional `.mcpb` setting; whether `import()` of a `data:` URL of the bundle's size works on Node 22; the VM's RAM and Postgres 16 plans, including the `EXISTS` form of the `changes` query and the pre-count; the real size of a customer's documents; whether a gap in a draft invoice series is acceptable to a chartered accountant. Each is a line in `DEPLOY.md` or the README, not silence.
